| FE-F17-14 | [ENH] | 🔵 | `PatientSets.tsx` (2,264 lines) · CLAUDE.md "Deliberate loader exceptions" | **The page is the documented loader exception, and the exception is what let FE-F17-2/-3/-6/-12 happen.** 29 `useState`, 30 HTTP calls, and server state (sets, doctors, and per-set batches, notes, photos) held in local state, loaded by hand-written `.then` chains. React Query fixes those four by construction: keys per work and per set, invalidation after writes, the bell invalidating. First paint waits for a sequential chain (sets → batches → notes, plus one PATCH per unread note → the R2 photo listing) before the spinner goes (runtime: about 250 ms on the LAN, 110–200 ms of it the R2 listing). Expanding a set waits for the same chain before opening, though per-section spinners exist. Suggested split: `SetCard`, `BatchList`, `SetCommunication`, `SetAttachments`, `useAlignerSet(workId)`. Smaller items: `React.memo` on a page with no props; manual `useCallback` in both drawer hooks (the React Compiler is on); the breadcrumb says "Back to Search" and goes to search for arrivals from the bell, the Works page or All Sets; `labNoteText` is one string shared by every set's open note box | ✅ RE1 |
| FE-F17-15 | [DEAD] | 🔵 | `PatientSets.tsx:75-143,166,196,207,345,1926,2264` · `router/loaders.ts:49-70,227-260` · `aligner.types.ts:10-17` · `utils/batchLastFlag.ts:20` | **Contract drift and stale comments.** Hand-written `MarkDeliveredResult`/`BatchStatusResult` shadow `deliverBatch.response`/`manufactureBatch.response`. `fetchJSON<{ doctors?: AlignerDoctorWithAliases[] }>` claims `id`/`name`/`logoPath`, which `alignerDoctorRow` doesn't declare; they arrive only because the row schema is loose. The local `Patient` view-model is built from the loader with six `as` casts, and `alignerPatientWorkLoader` casts its two contract reads to the hand-written `WorkData`/`PatientData` (FE-F2-9's shape). Four "Flat { success, sets } (no `data` key) → passthrough" comments are wrong (runtime: `{ success, data, timestamp }`). `aligner.types.ts` and the contract header cite the deleted `docs/shared-contract-progress.md`. Also: a static inline `style={{ marginBottom: '1.5rem' }}`; `LastFlagPrompt` is exported and imported nowhere; the wrapper classes `aligner-container` and `aligner-sets` have no rules | ✅ RE1 |

**FE-F17-2, -3, -6, -12 and -14 are one remediation.** Move the page's reads to React Query: `qk.aligner.sets(workId)`, plus per-set batches, notes and photos. Key the route elements by `workId`, and have every write (and the Portal-activity bell) invalidate the `['aligner']` prefix. The skip-if-loaded checks and the race then go away, and the lists refresh. CLAUDE.md's "deliberate loader exception" line for `PatientSets` would go in the same change.

**FE-F17-1 is the one that changes a contract.** `updateBatch` becomes partial (omitted = unchanged, like `updateSet`), and the three inline edits send one field each. No middleware changes, so the route-table snapshot stays the same. RB4's note on the work edit form is the same fix on F7's form, and RE could do both together.

**Owner calls:** FE-F17-9 (a) set cost on a non-USD work, after the three works are corrected; (b) may `clinical` set aligner prices. FE-F17-12: does opening a set count as reading the doctor's notes?

**Found along the way, not F17's to fix:**
- **Queued labels decide the logo from the doctor's own `logo_path`, but the PDF prints the server's default logo.** `PrintQueueContext.addToQueue` sets `includeLogo: !!doctor.logoPath`, so with 1 of 9 doctors carrying a logo, queued labels print no logo for the other eight. Meanwhile `/api/aligner/labels/generate` never receives a doctor's logo (`resolveLogoPath(undefined)` → `DEFAULT_LOGO_PATH`), so the one doctor with a logo gets the clinic's. Single-batch printing defaults the logo on. **F20.**
- **`AlignerDoctorsSettings.tsx:214`** has the placeholder `C:\Aligner_Sets\Labels\logo.png`, the same literal family as FE-F17-7. **F18.**
- **`components/react/CanvasControlButtons.module.css`** (and its `.d.ts`) is orphaned: the component was deleted in `483e3a0` (2026-06-04), and `frontend-dead-scan.mjs --css` now reports 1 orphan of 154. **F24.**

## F16 · Messaging (WhatsApp send/auth) · 2026-10-04

Scope read in full: `routes/WhatsAppSend` 466 · `routes/WhatsAppAuth` 95 · `components/react/SendMessage` 361 · `whatsapp-send/**` 700 (`MessageStatusTable` 219 · `DateSelector` 153 · `GroupSettings` 120 · `ActionButtons` 83 · `ConnectionStatus` 68 · `ProgressBar` 57) · `whatsapp-auth/**` 326 · `utils/whatsapp-api-client` 231 · `utils/whatsapp-validation` 107 · `utils/whatsapp-send-constants` 95 — 2,381 LOC — plus the two CSS modules (1,151 lines). RX1 rewrote three of F3's files after F3 read them, so these were re-read: `hooks/useWhatsAppAuth` 290 + `whatsappAuthMachine` 144, `useWhatsAppSync` 215, `useMessageStatus` 187, `useMessageCount` 102, `GlobalStateContext`'s WhatsApp half, `services/sse-whatsapp` + the `SseChannel` events it emits. Cross-checks the surface forced: every server route the screens call (`whatsapp.routes` send/resend/group-settings/send-appointment, `whatsapp-session.routes` initial-state/restart/refresh-qr/unlink/initialize, `whatsapp-media.routes#sendmedia2`, `messaging.routes` status/count/reset/message-text, `email.routes#send-appointments`), the batch sender and its counters (`whatsapp.ts#send`/`sendBatch`/`sendMessage`, `messageState.ts`, `app/whatsapp-events.ts`), the route table's role gates, `bookingError.ts#sendAppointmentConfirmation` (RC2's hand-down), and the git history of the two CSS regressions.

**Runtime: read-only against the live database.** A dev server on the live DB with the three CDC sinks, WhatsApp auto-init, LocalSend and the Cloudflare reconcile off by env; Playwright aborted every non-GET (none was attempted). Plus an in-memory probe of the real `messageState` module (no DB) and read-only greps of the prod logs. No screenshots; the probes live in the session scratchpad.

### Findings

| ID | Type | Sev | File | One-line | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FE-F16-1 | [BUG] | 🟠 | `services/messaging/messageState.ts:300-318,564-569` · `app/whatsapp-events.ts:65-70,97-102` · `hooks/useWhatsAppSync.ts:149-157` | **The send page's progress counts every WhatsApp message since the last restart, not this batch's.** `sent`/`failed` grow on every `MessageSent`/`MessageFailed`, including the one-off sends (payment receipts, booking confirmations, re-sends, task notices: `whatsapp.sendMessage` emits `MessageSent` too). `finishedSending` is only ever set to `true`; only `reset()` clears it, and that runs on a client restart/unlink/clear, never at batch start. Each progress frame carries those totals, and the page copies them over its own (`started` sets `total` = this batch, `sent` = 0). Probe (real `messageState` + the page's reducer steps): after 2 receipts, batch 1's **first** message shows **3/3** (bar full, 2 still to send); in batch 2 (same process), the first message's frame carries `finished: true`, so the page **ends the batch after one message**: bar hidden, Start enabled again, "✅ Completed! 6 delivered, 0 failed" for a 4-message batch. **Prod logs:** since the previous restart, 25 one-off messages went out before the 2026-09-29 batch and 33 before the 2026-10-03 batch (22 reminders each), so both batches showed about 26/22 → bar pinned at 100% for the whole run, and finished as "Completed! ~47–55 delivered". The `finished` carry-over needs two batches with no restart in between; the clinic's frequent deploys hide it today, a stable install won't. **Fix:** batch-scoped counters: zero `sent`/`failed`/`finished` when `sendBatch` starts, and count only the batch's own sends (or send the `MessageSession`'s numbers in the frame); the page takes `total` and `sent` from the frame | ✅ RE1 |
| FE-F16-2 | [BUG] | 🟡 | `hooks/useWhatsAppSync.ts:88-121` · `routes/api/whatsapp-session.routes.ts:145-163` | **Arriving mid-batch shows no batch.** `applyInitialState` reads `data.sendingProgress`, which `/api/wa/initial-state` has never sent. Runtime: the response has 14 top-level keys, none of them `sendingProgress`, and no `data` envelope. So the hook's whole REST re-sync is a no-op, and so is FE-F3-11's fix to it. Leave `/send` during a batch and come back, reload, or open a second tab: the later progress frames land on `started: false, total: 0`, so the bar never shows, the panel says "✅ WhatsApp client is ready!", and Start is enabled (the server answers `alreadyInProgress`). **Fix:** the server reports `{ started, finished, total, sent, failed }` for the running batch (`batchSendActive` + the session's total; needs FE-F16-1's per-batch counters) | ✅ RE1 |
| FE-F16-3 | [BUG] | 🟡 | `routes/api/whatsapp-session.routes.ts:181-201` · `hooks/useWhatsAppAuth.ts:192-208` · `ControlButtons.tsx:35-38` | **"Restart Client" waits for a restart that can't finish inside the request timeout, then reports a failure.** `POST /api/wa/restart` awaits `whatsapp.restart()` → `initializeInternal()`, which settles on ready or at `SESSION_RESTORATION_TIMEOUT` (120 s) / `FRESH_AUTH_TIMEOUT` (90 s); in QR mode only a scan resolves it. The route has the global 30 s timeout and the client 30 s. The button shows in AUTHENTICATED, RESTORING and ERROR. From ERROR with no saved session it is certain: the restart's QR arrives over SSE and is shown, then at 30 s the rejected POST dispatches `failed`, so the page drops the live QR for "Connection Error / Restart failed" until the next QR rotation (~20 s). A slow restore from AUTHENTICATED gets the same false failure. A 200 with `result: 'restart_failed'` is toasted as "restart initiated". `/refresh-qr`, `/unlink` and `/initialize` were made fire-and-forget for exactly this reason (the `/refresh-qr` comment says so). **Fix:** make `/restart` fire-and-forget too, and dispatch `reset` before the POST, as Refresh QR and Re-link do | ✅ RE1 |
| FE-F16-4 | [BUG] | 🟡 | `Dashboard.tsx:23,67` · `routes.config.tsx:529,551` · `GroupSettings.tsx:237-248` · route table `GET/PUT /api/wa/group-settings`, `POST /api/wa/{restart,refresh-qr,unlink,initialize}` (`admin\|front_desk`) · `whatsapp-session.routes.ts:83-85,160` | **Both WhatsApp screens are offered to `clinical`, who can use only part of them.** The Dashboard card and the routes have no role gate, and sending, resetting and emailing are `CLINICAL_ROLES`, but group settings and every pairing action are `admin\|front_desk`, and `initial-state` withholds the QR from non-finance roles (`mayPair`). A clinical user: every visit to `/send` toasts "Failed to load group settings: Insufficient permissions" and shows an editable form whose Save 403s. On `/auth` with an unpaired client, the machine settles into QR_REQUIRED with a `null` QR, so "Generating QR Code…" spins forever beside Refresh QR / Re-link buttons that 403. Same class as FE-F6-6 / FE-F7-7. **Fix:** a `roleCaps` flag (e.g. `manageWhatsApp`) that hides `GroupSettings` and the auth controls, and an explicit "pairing is done by the front desk" state on `/auth` | ✅ RE1 |
| FE-F16-5 | [BUG] | 🟡 | `SendMessage.tsx` (all of its markup) · commit `f424a75` (2025-11-18) | **The send-message page has had no styles since November 2025.** `send-message.css` (440 lines) was deleted as "Legacy HTML, not used by React", but `SendMessage` (`/send-message`, opened from the photo viewer's share button and the X-ray list) still uses its class names. Runtime: 9 of its 10 class/id hooks (`send-message-container`, `status-message`, `wainput`, `waform`, `auth-popup-btn`, `phone-input-wrapper`, `progress-container`, `#emptyBar`, `#filledBar`) match no rule in any loaded stylesheet; only `close-btn` hits a generic global. So the selects and inputs render at browser defaults, and the success, error and "authentication required" banners are identical unstyled text. **Fix:** a CSS module for the page (and drop the dead progress bar, FE-F16-15) | ✅ RE1 |
| FE-F16-6 | [BUG] | 🟡 | `SendMessage.tsx:114-155,346-348` · route table `POST /api/wa/sendmedia2 [… timeout(120000ms) …]` · `whatsapp-media.routes.ts:147-194` | **The send-message page can send a patient the same files twice.** (a) Send has no in-flight guard: it stays enabled during the POST, so a double click sends every file twice. (b) `postFormData('/api/wa/sendmedia2')` runs on the funnel's 30 s default while the route allows 120 s. A multi-file send, or a big Telegram file, that runs past 30 s is reported "Failed to send … timed out" while the server keeps sending, which invites a second send. This is FE-F1-2's class, fixed per call site; this site was missed. (c) The response's `result`/`error` describe only the **last** file, so with three files where only the last fails, the page shows only the error, not "2 of 3 sent". **Fix:** disable while sending, `timeoutMs: 120_000`, and report the per-file count either way | ✅ RE1 |
| FE-F16-7 | [BUG] | 🟡 | `WhatsAppSend.tsx:239-277,324-343` | **"Re-send all failed" is a client loop that outlives the page and isn't guarded across visits.** It posts one row every 2 s and keeps going after the user leaves `/send`. `bulkResending` is per-mount state, so on return the menu is enabled again, and a second "Re-send all failed" re-sends the rows the first loop hasn't reached: duplicates to patients. The single-row "Re-send message" has no in-flight guard either, and the row stays FAILED until the next status refetch, so it can be fired twice. **Fix:** a server-side bulk re-send with an in-progress guard (like the batch), or at least a module-scoped in-flight set keyed by `appointmentId` | ✅ RE1 |
| FE-F16-8 | [ENH] | 🟡 | `utils/whatsapp-api-client.ts` · `utils/whatsapp-validation.ts` · `useMessageCount.ts:56-72` · `WhatsAppSend.tsx:141-209` · `types/api.types.ts:151-165` | **The bespoke `APIClient` (~340 LOC with its validator) duplicates the funnel and bypasses the contracts.** Two of its four calls are `sendData` envelopes with contracts: `GET /messaging/count/:date` (runtime: `{success, data, timestamp}`) and `POST /messaging/reset/:date`. The page reads them through hand-written shapes: `MessageCountData` + `validateMessageCountResponse`, and `WhatsAppResetResponse`. Their doc comments name the wrong paths (`/api/messages/reset/:date`; `EmailResponse` says `/api/email/send/:date`). The other two responses, from `/wa/send` and `/email/send-appointments`, are raw but funnel-compatible: `unwrapEnvelope` passes a body with no `data` key through unchanged, so `alreadyInProgress` and `appointmentCount` still read at the top level. Retiring it removes the second CSRF/timeout/retry implementation, the one that produced FE-F1-4. The server responses stay raw; nothing changes on the wire | ✅ RE1 |
| FE-F16-9 | [BUG] | 🔵 | `bookingError.ts:66-79` · `AppointmentCalendar.tsx:374-385` · `whatsapp.routes.ts:289-325` | **Arabic booking forms show the server's English WhatsApp-confirmation failures** (RC2's hand-down). `POST /api/wa/send-appointment` answers 200 `{success: false, message}` with "WhatsApp not connected", "Appointment not found", "No phone number for patient" or "Invalid phone number", and `sendAppointmentConfirmation` toasts the `message` verbatim beside translated chrome. **Fix:** a `code` per case, mapped to `form.wa*` keys; the English calendar uses the same codes | ✅ RE1 |
| FE-F16-10 | [DEAD] | 🔵 | `MessageStatusTable.tsx:41-57,166-200` · `useMessageStatus.ts:44-99,113` · `messaging.contract.ts:20-36` | **The status table's row types are written by hand, and its Message column is always empty.** `transformedMessage` is a closed `z.object` with no `message` field (removed on purpose; the text comes from `message-text`). So `MessageItem`'s fallbacks (`message`/`messageText`/`content`, `phoneNumber`/`mobile`, `patient`, `sentAt`/`timestamp`) and `Message`'s legacy fields (`PatientID`, `patient_name`, `Phone`, `MessageText`, `SentAt`) can never be set, and `fetchJSON<{ messages?: Message[] }>` sits over the contract's own type. Runtime: today's 22 rows, 0 non-empty Message cells. Also: `escapeHtml` → `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` ×3, which round-trips to the same text React would render escaped anyway, passed hook → page → table as a prop with `MESSAGE_STATUS_TEXT`/`MESSAGE_STATUS_CLASS`/`formatDisplayDate`. **Fix:** drop the column (or fill it on demand from `message-text`), type the rows from the contract, render plain text | ✅ RE1 |
| FE-F16-11 | [BUG] | 🔵 | `WhatsAppSend.tsx:112-209` · `ProgressBar.tsx:27,53` · `ConnectionStatus.tsx:21-28` · `ActionButtons.tsx:48` · `whatsapp.ts:1812-1821` | **The send page's status is stale or overstated around a batch.** (a) The count banner isn't refreshed when a batch finishes (only after the unconfirmed warning, a reset or a re-send), so it still says "22 messages ready to send" after all 22 went out. F2's map files `qk.whatsapp.messageCount` as SSE-driven, but no event refreshes it. (b) Start isn't disabled at a zero count: it toasts "Messages sending started", and nothing follows, because the server's no-messages path emits `finished` without `started`. A send that fails to start (circuit breaker open) also leaves only that toast. (c) "{sent} of {total} messages delivered" and "Completed! N delivered" count server acceptance, the exact misreading the zero-ack warning exists to correct. (d) The bar counts only `sent`, so failures stall it short of 100%. (e) The Reset/Email confirm timers are never cleared: a second arm within 3 s is disarmed early by the first timer, and a third click while a send is in flight fires again (`send-appointments` now answers 409) | ✅ RE1 |
| FE-F16-12 | [BUG] | 🔵 | `useWhatsAppSync.ts:125-131` · `useWhatsAppAuth.ts:95-104` · `services/sse-channel.ts:137,183-190` · `ConnectionStatus.tsx:170-180` · `whatsappAuthMachine.ts:121-124` · `ConnectionStatusFooter.tsx:19-38` | **Neither WhatsApp screen can show a dropped stream.** Both hooks listen for `disconnected`, which `SseChannel` emits only when the refcount reaches zero, and the app-wide `WhatsAppStatusProvider` never lets it. Neither listens for `reconnecting`, which is what a drop emits. So "Disconnected from server" and the auth page's DISCONNECTED are unreachable. The send panel also ranks the (stale) `clientReady` above `error`. On the auth page, every reopen sends the machine from QR_REQUIRED to CONNECTED, which renders nothing, while the footer shows a green "Connected": the QR blinks out until `initial-state` answers, and stays blank if that read fails. The footer has no case for RESTORING or NEEDS_RELINK, so it reads "Unknown" during a restore that can take two minutes | ✅ RE1 |
| FE-F16-13 | [BUG] | 🔵 | `SuccessDisplay.tsx:9-14` · `WhatsAppSend.tsx:360-371` · `useWhatsAppAuth.ts:265-276` · `SendMessage.tsx:236-260` | **Leftovers from the old redirect contract.** `SuccessDisplay` shows "Redirecting you back to the messaging page…" only for a `?returnTo` param that nothing sets (the hook redirects via `location.state.returnPath`), so the 2 s redirect happens under "ready to send messages" copy. `WhatsAppSend`'s `?authCompleted` effect is dead: nothing sets the param. The send-message page's "Authenticate WhatsApp" opens `/auth` in a popup, which after pairing navigates to the full `/send` page (the default `returnPath`) instead of closing. Its auth-required banner doesn't clear when `clientReady` flips live, and "Check Again" reloads the page | ✅ RE1 |
| FE-F16-14 | [BUG] | 🔵 | `GroupSettings.tsx:99` · `SendMessage.tsx:57-70,269-271` | **Identity and registry literals.** The group-name placeholder is "Shwan Orthodontics" on every install. The send-message page hardcodes the Google contact sources (`shw` "Dr. Shwan Phone", `cli` "Clinic Phone") instead of mapping `GOOGLE_CONTACT_ACCOUNTS` as `TelegramShareModal` does, so an account added to the registry never appears. It also casts the contact reads `as ContactData[]`; the Google read is `z.array(z.unknown())`, so nothing checks a row, and patients with no phone (1,349 of 6,855 on the live DB) are listed as "Name - null" | ✅ RE1 |
| FE-F16-15 | [DEAD] | 🔵 | `whatsapp-send-constants.ts:7-30,48-55` · `whatsapp-api-client.ts:17-22,52-89,218-230` · `whatsapp-validation.ts:57-79,84-98` · `SendMessage.tsx:350-355` · `WhatsAppSend.module.css:272-295,364` · `ConnectionStatus.tsx:43-54` · 3 duplicate types | **Dead code.** 9 of `CONFIG`'s 15 keys have no reader (`DEDUPLICATION_WINDOW_MS`, `PROGRESS_BAR_*` ×2, `HEARTBEAT_INTERVAL_MS`, `ERROR_DISPLAY_DURATION_MS`, `MAX_RECONNECT_ATTEMPTS`, `CLIENT_RESTART_DELAY_MS`, which `useWhatsAppAuth` defines again, `LOGOUT_DELAY_MS`, `DEBOUNCE_DELAY_MS`). Also unused: `UI_STATES.SENDING`/`COMPLETED`; `APIClient.cancelRequest`/`cancelAllRequests`, which have no callers, and the exported `RetryManager`/`RetryOptions`, which are internal; `validateApiResponse`'s array branch; the send-message page's `#emptyBar/#filledBar`, a progress bar nothing drives. Types written twice: `MessageSummary`, `SendingProgress`, and `InitialStateResponse` (with a third inline copy in `GlobalStateContext`). CSS: the `.messageCountInfo.{loading,error,success,warning}` modifiers are never applied. The module keeps a "Connection Status States (keep as global…)" comment with no rules under it, while `ConnectionStatus` emits four `connection-status-*` classes that nothing styles (runtime: 0 rules), so connected, error and sending all look the same. Those rules were lost in the Jan-2026 CSS-module conversion (`a87a64e`). `validateDate` accepts any string `Date` can parse (`1`, `May 5`, `2026-02-30`) for `/send?date=` | ✅ RE1 |
| FE-F16-16 | [ENH] | 🔵 | `GlobalStateContext.tsx:154-182` · `useWhatsAppSync.ts:109-128,181-186` · `useWhatsAppAuth.ts:74-82,95-98,126-130,148-155,243-253` | **`/api/wa/initial-state` is fetched by three independent readers.** On a page load, `GlobalStateContext`, `useWhatsAppSync` (single-flight) and `useWhatsAppAuth` (no single-flight; `connected` and `ensureConnected().then` can both fire) each GET it. Each call renders the QR data URL and, while a viewer is connected, emits `whatsapp_initialization_requested`. After FE-F16-2, the send hook's read is pure overhead. **Fix:** one `queryOptions` (`qk.whatsapp.initialState`) that the three read and the transport `connected` event invalidates | ✅ RE1 |

**FE-F16-1 → FE-F16-2 → FE-F16-11(a–d) are one remediation.** Per-batch counters on the server make the frames truthful (1). Reporting them in `initial-state` lets a late tab join (2). The page's copy and banner then follow from the frame (11). Verifying it needs a paired WhatsApp client: do it with a test number on the throwaway install, never the clinic's account.

**FE-F16-3 and FE-F16-4 touch the route table.** FE-F16-3 changes no middleware (the handler stops awaiting), so the snapshot stays the same. FE-F16-4 is client-only unless the owner decides `clinical` should manage the group, which would move two routes to `CLINICAL_ROLES`. **Owner call:** should doctors and assistants pair WhatsApp and edit the group, or should the screens hide those parts from them?

**Found along the way, not F16's to fix:**
- **Task-assignment WhatsApp messages hardcode the clinic's name**: `services/messaging/task-notify.ts:45` opens every message with "Shwan Orthodontics" as a literal, not a fallback. Backend, same family as RX1's schedule-email note; it should read `getClinicNames()`.
- **Other screens' clinic literals, for their sessions:** `PatientSets.tsx:2138` "Shwan Lab" as the lab-note author (**F17**); the `EmailSettings.tsx:307` and `GeneralSettings.tsx:575,590` placeholders, and `C:\ShwanOrtho\ProtocolHandlers.ini` in `ProtocolHandlersSettings` (**F21/F22**); `PortalLogin.tsx:24,73` title and subtitle (**F23**).

## F15 · Slideshow · 2026-10-01

Scope read in full: `slideshow/**` 2,256 LOC (`SlideshowBuilder` 580 · `PatientSlideshow` 392 · `SlideshowPlayer` 362 · `ManageConfigsModal` 201 · `AnnotationCanvas` 174 · `configResolver` 172 · `SaveConfigModal` 110 · `photoTypes` 69 · `FolderPickerModal` 58 · `useWakeLock` 56 · `useFullscreen` 42 · `types` 40), plus its four CSS modules (1,133 lines). Cross-checks the surface forced: the page's mount in `ContentRenderer` (no `key`), `UniversalHeader`'s last-view navigation, `PatientFolderPicker` (F6's) with `files/fileHelpers#buildContentUrl`, `query/client.ts`'s defaults, the `galleryQuery`/`timepointsQuery`/`slideshowConfigsQuery` factories and `qk.slideshow`, `ToastContext`'s stability, and the other view-label tables (`GridComponent`, `compare/types.ts`, `shared/photo-views.ts`). Contracts: `slideshow.contract.ts`, `patient.contract` timepoints + gallery. Server halves: `slideshow.routes.ts`, `slideshow-config-queries.ts`, the `slideshow_configs` DDL, its CDC trigger and its two seeded templates in the baseline migration, the 2026-07-21 Supabase mirror migration, `timepoint-queries.ts#getTimePoints` (date, then code), `utils/file-mime.ts` (what counts as an image), `thumbnail.service.ts` (allowed widths), the `/DolImgs` cache header (curl through the dev proxy: `private, max-age=31536000, immutable`), and `CLINICAL_ROLES`.

**Runtime-verified on a throwaway install; nothing was written to the clinic's data or photo volume.** PostgreSQL 18 from the session scratchpad (F14's extracted binaries, a fresh cluster on port 55432, `Asia/Baghdad`), set up with `db:migrate` + `db:setup` ("Bright Smile Orthodontics") + `db:seed:demo`; `MACHINE_PATH` pointed at an empty scratch folder and a wrapper blanked every `.env` key. Express `:3101` + Vite `:5273`. **Writes, all on the throwaway:** one session and two renders for demo patient 3 (to make a one-session patient), one TIFF in patient 1's folder, one render overwritten with a smaller version (put back), one render moved away (put back), two saved presentations, and one seeded template deleted. Seven Playwright probes: the player (a wake-lock mock that releases on hide the way browsers do), saved presentations, annotation and keys, drag and phone width, the no-fullscreen fallback at 412 px, the template cache, and an in-app history step between two patients. **Live data, read-only** (owner's standing go-ahead): SQL inside `BEGIN READ ONLY … ROLLBACK`, and a file listing of `C:\clinic1`. The probe scripts lived in the scratchpad.

### Findings

| ID | Type | Sev | File | One-line | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FE-F15-1 | [BUG] | 🟡 | `SlideshowPlayer.module.css:188-214,262-271` · `SlideshowPlayer.tsx:186-216,338-346` | **While the player's controls are hidden they still take taps, so a tap in the top-right corner closes the presentation.** The controls hide by `opacity: 0` with `pointer-events: none` on their container, but the top bar and the dots set `pointer-events: auto` on themselves, which overrides the inherited `none`, and the invisible bar sits above the gesture layer. Runtime, 1280×900, after the 2.8 s auto-hide: the element under the ✕'s position is still the ✕. A tap in the top-right corner, which is the *next* zone, closed the player and left fullscreen. A tap at (120, 30), the *previous* zone, did nothing and didn't bring the controls back (still 3 / 4); the same tap at mid-height went to 2 / 4. At 412 px the five buttons also cover the centre of the top strip (the element at (206, 20) is a player button), so on a phone a tap near the top toggles framing, transition, caption or annotate unseen. Closing loses the place: *Play* starts again at slide 1. The dots strip swallows taps the same way. **Fix:** `visibility: hidden` on the hidden controls (it removes both the hit target and the tab stop), revealed on any key | ✅ fixed (RD) |
| FE-F15-2 | [BUG] | 🟡 | `useWakeLock.ts:22-55` | **After a switch to another app or tab, the wake lock is never taken again, so the screen can sleep mid-presentation.** The browser releases a screen wake lock whenever the page is hidden. The hook re-acquires on `visibilitychange`, but `acquire()` returns early while `lockRef.current` is set, and nothing clears the ref when the browser releases the lock (the sentinel's `release` event isn't listened for). Runtime, with a wake-lock mock that releases on hide like a browser: after *Play*, a lock is held; after hidden → visible, **none** is held and no new request is made. Wherever the player stays open across the switch, the display dims and sleeps at the device's timeout for the rest of the presentation. On an iPhone it always stays open, because Safari has no element fullscreen and the player is a plain overlay. A smaller gap: a cleanup that runs while `request()` is still pending (dev StrictMode, or a close within milliseconds) releases nothing, and the lock that resolves afterwards is kept until the page is hidden (dev: 2 requests on *Play*, both held). **Fix:** clear `lockRef` in the sentinel's `release` listener (or test `sentinel.released`), and release a lock that resolves after cleanup | ✅ fixed (RD) |
| FE-F15-3 | [BUG] | 🟡 | `configResolver.ts:62-96,130-172` · `SaveConfigModal.tsx:90-94` | **Generic templates resolve *first* and *latest* to the earliest and the newest session by date, whatever is in them.** (a) **A patient with one session gets the same photo twice, as a before/after pair, with a success toast.** `first` and `latest` are then the same session, both photos resolve to the same file, and nothing counts as missing. Every install ships two such templates, *Smile — Initial & Final* and *Profile — Initial & Final*, seeded by the baseline migration; they are the only two configs in the live database. Runtime, on a one-session demo patient: the slide is `300.i13` + `300.i13`, the toast reads "Applied “Smile — Initial & Final”", and both captions read "Initial · Smile · 10-05-2026". Live (by view rows): 802 of the 1,881 photographed patients have one session; 776 of them have a *Smile* and 767 a *Profile*. (b) **A template saved from one session always becomes *first*.** `toTemplatePayload` maps the earliest session in the timeline to `first`, and with one session that is the only one. Runtime: a timeline of the *Final* session's Smile (`103.i13`) saved as a template stored `{"tp":"first","type":"i13"}`, and applying it showed the *Initial* Smile (`100.i13`). The dialog's hint ("matched by photo type & first/latest session") doesn't say which end a session becomes. A two-session timeline built from *Initial* and a *Progress* session applies as *Initial* + the newest session, the same way. (c) *latest* is the newest session even when it lacks the view. For 279 of the 1,079 multi-session patients the newest session has no *Smile*; for 29 of them an earlier progress session has one, but the template reports it "not available". **Fix:** resolve `latest` to the newest session that has the view; skip and count a slide whose `first` and `latest` resolve to the same photo; let a one-session template say which end it means (store `latest` when it was built from the patient's newest session, or ask) | ✅ fixed (RD) |
| FE-F15-4 | [BUG] | 🟡 | `PatientSlideshow.tsx:50-78,102,170-187,287-292` · `configResolver.ts:100-126` | **The slideshow fixes each photo's URL when it is added or saved. A re-rendered photo then plays in its old version, and a removed one plays as a grey placeholder after "Applied".** (a) **The timeline.** Each slide's `url` (`/DolImgs/{name}?v={mtime}`) is kept in sessionStorage as it was when the photo was added. After the photo is re-rendered in the editor, the palette gets the new `?v`, but the timeline keeps the old one, which the browser holds as `immutable` for a year. Runtime: `100.i10` added, then the file overwritten with a 600-px version (what a re-render does). After a reload, the palette's *Profile* was `?v=…8380` at 600 px, while the timeline chip was still `?v=…8343` at **780 px**, and *Play* showed the 780-px version. The chip still counts as the same photo (`×1` on the palette tile), so nothing hints that two versions are in play. This is the natural sequence: build, notice a framing to fix, fix it in the editor, come back, play. (b) **Saved presentations** store the file name and rebuild `/DolImgs/{name}` with **no** version (FE-F13-1's shape, handed down by F13 as `configResolver.ts:120`). Their captions are the session name and date at the time of saving. Nothing checks that the photos still exist. Runtime: a saved two-slide presentation whose first photo (`101.i10`) had been moved away applied with "Applied “Lit test”", and slide 1 was the placeholder. Templates, by contrast, count and report missing photos. (c) The galleries behind all this are fetched with `fetchJSON` into `useState`, outside React Query, although `galleryQuery` (`qk.patient.gallery`) exists and the grid and the editor use it, so the slideshow keeps its own copy that nothing invalidates. Live: no presentation has been saved yet (0 literal configs), so (b) has no victims today. **Fix:** keep only identity in the timeline and in saved presentations (`tp` + `name`, or the folder `path`), and build the URL at render time from `galleryQuery` (name + `mtime`). That also tells *Apply* which photos are gone, to report them the way templates do | ✅ fixed (RD) |
| FE-F15-5 | [BUG] | 🔵 | `FolderPickerModal.tsx:28-40` · `PatientFolderPicker.tsx:42` · `utils/file-mime.ts:41-45` | ***Add from folder* offers TIFF and HEIC files, which Chrome and Edge can't display, so they play as placeholders.** The picker lists every file in the `image` category, which includes `.tif`, `.tiff`, `.heic` and `.heif`. Its tiles are the server's WebP thumbnails, so a TIFF looks fine there, but the slide points at the raw file (`files/content?path=…`, no `thumb`). Runtime: `scan-2024.tif` showed a 208-px thumbnail in the picker; once added, its chip and its slide were `/images/placeholder.svg`. Live (read-only listing of `C:\clinic1` to depth 4, `working` excluded): **476** `.tif` files in **265** patients' folders, 284 of them at a patient folder's root, where the picker opens; no HEIC. **Fix:** show those formats through the server's converter (`thumb=2048`, the size the editor already uses), or leave them out of this picker | ✅ fixed (RD) |
| FE-F15-6 | [BUG] | 🔵 | `AnnotationCanvas.tsx:68-114` · `SlideshowPlayer.tsx:293,302-307` | **Annotations are drawn in screen pixels, not on the photo, so a layout change leaves them pointing at the wrong place.** The canvas covers the whole stage and stores points relative to it. Its ResizeObserver repaints them at the same pixels, while the photo, which is `object-fit: contain`, moves and scales. Runtime, 1280×900: a circle drawn a quarter of the way into the photo, at (445, 225). After *Toggle framing* (Reel 9:16, which stays reachable while annotating), the photo was 506×584 at (387, 158) and the ink was still at (445, 225), now 11 % into the photo; the circled point had moved to (513, 304). Turning a tablet does the same. Also, `onPointerMove` doesn't check `pointerId`. With two fingers, or a palm beside a stylus on a touch screen without palm rejection, both pointers' moves go into one stroke as a zigzag, and lifting the first ends the other's stroke. **Fix:** store points as fractions of the displayed photo's rectangle and repaint from those; keep one stroke per pointer | ✅ fixed (RD) |
| FE-F15-7 | [BUG] | 🔵 | `PatientSlideshow.tsx:136-154` · `keys.ts:308-310` | **A template change reaches only the current patient's list.** The three writes invalidate `qk.slideshow.list(personId)`, but a generic template is in every patient's list. Runtime: patient 1's slideshow was opened, then patient 3's, where *Profile — Initial & Final* was deleted. Back on patient 1 within the 30 s `staleTime`, the *Apply* menu still listed it and no request was made, and applying it works from the cached row. Renames and new templates lag the same way. F2's map listed `slideshow.list` as never invalidated, and correctly so; both halves are wrong (corrected there). **Fix:** invalidate `qk.slideshow.all()` (one of F2's zero-reference keys) after a template write | ✅ fixed (RD) |
| FE-F15-8 | [BUG] | 🔵 | `ContentRenderer.tsx:237-242` · `PatientSlideshow.tsx:156-187` · `SlideshowBuilder.tsx:99-101,145-165` | **Latent: on a change of patient, another patient's photos can be listed under this patient's session and added to their timeline.** It is FE-F13-12's shape, with a worse outcome. The page isn't keyed by patient. Its own reset (`pidRef`) clears the timeline and the gallery cache, but the builder keeps which sessions were open, and a gallery read still in flight for the old patient lands in the new patient's cache under the same session code. Runtime: with patient 1's *Initial* gallery delayed 2.5 s, an in-app history step to `/patient/3/slideshow`. Under patient 3's header, patient 3's *Initial* session opened by itself ("Loading…"), then listed `100.i10` … `100.i21`: **patient 1's eight photos**. One tap put `100.i10` into patient 3's timeline and into `slideshow_seq_3`, from which *Save* would store it as patient 3's presentation. **No UI path produces that step today**, as F13 found for Compare: the header's patient tab reopens the same patient, and search goes through `/patient-management`. It becomes a 🔴 on the day one does. **Fix (one line, with FE-F13-12's):** `key={personId}` on the page; the `pidRef` block can then go | ✅ fixed (RD) |
| FE-F15-9 | [ENH] | 🔵 | `SlideshowPlayer.tsx:146-164,181-216` · `SlideshowBuilder.tsx:346-383,499-505` · `AnnotationCanvas.tsx:138-148` | **Keyboard, remote and screen-reader gaps.** (a) Presenter remotes send PageDown/PageUp, which the player ignores. Runtime: PageDown left 1 / 3 and PageUp left 2 / 3, while → moved. (b) Every mouse button navigates. The gesture layer doesn't check `button`, so a right-click in the right third advances (and opens the context menu). (c) Reordering and pairing are pointer-only. The chip grip can't be focused, so a keyboard user can only remove photos and add them again. (d) The *Apply* menu is a `role="menu"` with no focus on open, no arrow keys and no Escape (FE-F12-13(b)'s shape). (e) Slide changes aren't announced (the counter isn't a live region). Focus is on `<body>` after *Play* (runtime) and doesn't return to *Play* on close. The colour swatches are named by hex code ("Color #ff3b30"). In fullscreen, focus does stay inside the player (runtime: Shift+Tab cycles its five buttons) | ✅ fixed (RD) |
| FE-F15-10 | [ENH] | 🔵 | `SlideshowBuilder.tsx:448-454,506-514,559-563` · `PatientSlideshow.tsx:179` · `FolderPickerModal.tsx:34` | **The builder downloads full-size photos to fill thumbnails** (FE-F14-12's shape). The palette tiles, the timeline chips and the drag ghost all use the slide's own URL. For session photos that is the full render (live median 2.2 MB, F13's numbers); for folder photos it is the camera original. Opening a patient's four sessions downloads 32 renders, about 70 MB at the median, before anything plays. `loading="lazy"` helps only below the fold. The working-files endpoint already serves 480-px WebP thumbnails of the same files, and the folder picker uses 240-px ones. **Fix:** thumbnail URLs in the builder, and the full file only in the player | ✅ fixed (RD) |
| FE-F15-11 | [BUG] | 🔵 | `PatientSlideshow.tsx:35-40,50-70,113-123` · `types.ts:3-7` | **Casts over a contracted read, and stored data used unchecked** (FE-F12-15's shape). The timepoints read is cast to a hand-written `TimepointApiRow` that repeats the contract's three fields, then mapped into a second hand-written `Timepoint`. The stored timeline is `JSON.parse`d and cast `as SlideItem` with no check, so a stored entry missing `url` or `tp` reaches the builder and the player as `undefined`. **Fix:** use the contract's row type; validate the stored timeline (it is a boundary) and drop entries that don't parse | ✅ fixed (RD) |
| FE-F15-12 | [DEAD] | 🔵 | `photoTypes.ts:25-34,49-52` · `PatientSlideshow.tsx:1-9,55-66,113` · `slideshow.contract.ts:1-4` | **Leftovers.** `isLogoImage` filters out a logo slot the gallery payload no longer has: the contract is a closed object of the eight views ("the centre logo is a client-only layout concern"). `readSession` still folds the legacy `id` and `second` fields, retired 2026-06-28; sessionStorage is per tab, so no such entry can be left (FE-F6-14's reasoning). The page's docblock says "Frontend-only: reuses the existing timepoints/gallery endpoints", but saved presentations have their own table and four routes. The contract's docblock says the table is LOCAL-ONLY, and so do the route's and the query module's (see below), but it has been mirrored, forward-only, since 2026-07-21 (`mirror-approvals-slideshow`, and the CDC trigger in the baseline). The view labels exist three times: `PHOTO_TYPE_LABELS` here (which the editor also uses), the grid's cell `alt`s, and Compare's `PHOTO_TYPES`, while `shared/photo-views.ts` holds the codes. They agree today. There is also one hand-written `useMemo`, under React Compiler | ✅ fixed (RD) |

**FE-F15-4 is the slideshow's half of RD's photo-URL work.** Compare (FE-F13-1, -5, -8) and the slideshow both want their names and versions from `galleryQuery`. With that in place, the editor's render-complete invalidation (FE-F14-2's fix) reaches the slideshow as well. FE-F15-10 is then a second URL from the same entry. FE-F15-8 rides with FE-F13-12's `key={personId}`.

**FE-F15-1: why it went unnoticed.** A mouse rarely goes to the top edge while the controls are hidden, and in a desktop check the controls are usually visible. On a phone in Reel framing, where the photo fills the screen and the hidden bar spans most of the top strip, it is easy to hit.

**Found along the way, not F15's to fix:**
- **Two more stale "LOCAL-ONLY" docblocks:** `routes/api/slideshow.routes.ts:1-9` and `slideshow-config-queries.ts:1-7` (backend; the contract's is in FE-F15-12). Fix them in the same change.
- **The two seeded templates are rows in the baseline migration, with English names.** Every install, an Arabic-language clinic included, starts with *Smile — Initial & Final* and *Profile — Initial & Final*, and any staff role can delete them permanently after one confirm (writes are `CLINICAL_ROLES`, i.e. all three roles). Whether they belong in `db:setup` with the other clinic content is for **F21**.

## F14 · Photo editor + sharing · 2026-10-01

Scope read in full: `photo-editor/**` 1,939 (`PhotoEditor` 538 · `SequenceSidebar` 338 · `SlotCanvas` 210 · `SlotGrid` 207 · `photoEditorTypes` 196 · `usePhotoEditorState` 119 · `RenameFolderModal` 112 · `SlotActions` 87 · `SlotContextMenu` 69 · `SlotToolbar` 63) · `share/**` 461 (`TelegramShareModal` 354 · `ShareSheet` 107) · `localsend/LocalSendShareModal` 383 · `services/photo-render-watch` 102 — 2,885 LOC — plus their eleven CSS modules (1,247 lines). Cross-checks the surface forced: the editor's three entry points (`GridComponent#handleReimport`, the `PhotoSessionDialog` call sites in the grid, `Navigation` and `ViewPatientInfo`) and its mount in `ContentRenderer` (`key={tpCode}`); `hooks/useImportFolder` and the dialog's *Import folder* block; `hooks/useUnsavedRouteGuard`; `services/sse-channel.ts` and `sse-appointments.ts`; `query/client.ts`'s retry policy and the `telegramSendProgressQuery`/`patientPhonesQuery` factories; the three `ShareSheet` render sites (grid lightbox, Files, Compare). Contracts: `photo-editor`, `telegram`, `localsend`, `share`, `file-explorer` (upload/folder/rename). Server halves: `photo-editor.routes.ts` (prepare, render with its background job and SSE announce, view delete), `native-timepoint-queries.ts#findOrCreateNativeTimePoint`, `photo-original-tags.ts`, `photo-render.service.ts` (EXIF auto-orient, `deleteWorkingView`), `thumbnail.service.ts`, `sse-broadcaster.ts#onPhotoTimepointRendered`, `telegram.routes.ts` (job store, TTLs), `localsend.routes.ts` + `localsend.service.ts` (discovery, probe, transfer, `timedFetch`), `share-ref.ts`, `file-explorer.routes.ts` (upload), `patient-assets.service.ts` (who reads `OPG`), `utils/phone-formatter.ts`, `utils/fetch-timeout.ts`, and the route-table gates for all of them.

**Runtime-verified on a throwaway install; nothing was written to the clinic's data or photo volume.** PostgreSQL 18 ran from the session scratchpad (port 55432, `Asia/Baghdad`), set up with `db:migrate` + `db:setup` ("Bright Smile Orthodontics") + `db:seed:demo`. Patient #1 has four sessions, each with eight renders and eight view-tagged originals. `MACHINE_PATH` pointed at an empty ext4 scratch folder, and a wrapper blanked every `.env` key, so no sink, WhatsApp, Telegram, LocalSend, Supabase or Cloudflare setting reached the run. Express `:3101` + Vite `:5273`. **Writes, all on the throwaway:** two view removes, one session re-date, two renders into a stale session, one render with its source moved away (put back afterwards), one *Rename folder*. Telegram and LocalSend were exercised by Playwright `page.route` mocks (their server flags were off). The LocalSend probe messages came from calling the service directly against an unroutable and a closed address. **Live data, read-only** (owner's standing go-ahead, see [[live-data-read-ok]] in memory): SQL inside `BEGIN READ ONLY … ROLLBACK`, a directory listing of the patient folders under `C:\clinic1`, and the production `combined*.log`. The probe scripts lived in the scratchpad.

### Findings

| ID | Type | Sev | File | One-line | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FE-F14-1 | [BUG] | 🟡 | `LocalSendShareModal.tsx:58-102` · `ShareSheet.tsx:60-62` | **The LocalSend picker opens empty: it never asks the server for devices.** The device scan runs from an adjust-during-render block keyed on `useState(open)`, so it fires only when `open` changes from `false` to `true` on a mounted modal. All three share entry points (grid lightbox, Files, Compare) go through `ShareSheet`, which mounts `LocalSendShareModal` with `open` already `true`, so the block never runs. Runtime: choosing *LocalSend* sent **0** `GET /api/localsend/devices` and showed "No devices found yet. Try Rescan or Add by IP.". With a device mocked into the server's list, it was still not shown until *Rescan*. With the server's LocalSend off, the "LocalSend is disabled on the server" notice also stays hidden until *Rescan* (`enabled` starts `true`). **A regression:** until `1a225e2` (2026-06-15, the set-state-in-effect sweep) the scan ran in a `useEffect([open])`, which fires on mount. LocalSend is in production use: 89 transfers in the retained logs (10 Jun–15 Sep), each since 15 Jun one *Rescan* away. The server announces every 5 s and keeps what it hears for 5 min, so a plain read on open would list the devices at once. Two smaller gaps: `?rescan=1` answers before the new announce has replies (`scan()` is fire-and-forget), so a device that answers only that rescan appears on the next click; and the devices and transfer state live in `useState` (CLAUDE.md: no server state in local state). **Fix:** a `useQuery` for the devices (`enabled: open`), with *Rescan* = the `?rescan=1` read plus one delayed refetch | ✅ fixed (RD) |
| FE-F14-2 | [BUG] | 🟡 | `PhotoEditor.tsx:172-219,354-370` · `SequenceSidebar.tsx:89-96` · `usePhotoEditorState.ts:25-43` | **In the editor, a removed photo comes back in its slot at once, and the photos grid keeps showing it.** *Remove* on a saved slot deletes the render and untags its original on the server. On the client it clears the slot and re-lists the sidebar folder, but it never invalidates the gallery. The re-listed folder is a new input to the hydration effect, which re-seeds every slot without a live edit from the **cached** gallery, deleted file included. Runtime: tp0 *Profile* → *Remove* → `working/100.i10` gone on disk and the original back to `demo-photo.jpg`, yet the slot shows `/DolImgs/100.i10?v=…` again (`naturalWidth` 780, from the browser's immutable cache), and its menu reads *Remove · Original missing — drag one to redo*. Then the sidebar's *Photos* link, in-app: the grid showed the removed *Rest* thumbnail (`100.i12`) a second later, with **0** gallery requests. Neither *Remove* nor the render-complete handlers refresh Compare's `timepointImages` or the `workingFiles` list (F13 recorded this from the reader's side). **Fix:** after *Remove* invalidate the gallery, together with FE-F12-3's `invalidatePatientPhotos(personId)`; call the same helper on render completion | ✅ fixed (RD) |
| FE-F14-3 | [BUG] | 🟡 | `PhotoEditor.tsx:304-349` · `ContentRenderer.tsx:310-321` · `photo-editor.routes.ts:205-210` · `native-timepoint-queries.ts#findOrCreateNativeTimePoint` | **The editor saves into the session whose name and date are in its URL, not the session it shows.** The URL carries `tp{code}?tpName=…&date=…`. Hydration reads the gallery by code, but *Save* posts only `{ tpName, tpDate, slots }`, and the server finds or **creates** a session by name and date. Any stale URL therefore saves somewhere else: browser Back/Forward after *Edit name & date*, an editor left open in another tab, or another user's re-date. Runtime: the editor for tp1 (*Progress*, 30 Mar 2025) was opened, then tp1 was re-dated to 29 Mar, then Back. The editor still read *Progress 2025-03-30*, and its sidebar said "Folder is not created yet." because the folder had been renamed with the session. One original was dragged into *Profile*, then *Save* → **202 `tp_code: 4`**: a new session *Progress 30 Mar 2025* was created and `104.i10` written into it. The page then navigated to `/photos/tp1`, which has no new photo, and 105 s later toasted "Photo save is taking longer than expected — check the photos grid", because the watchdog waits on tp1. The view delete has the same split: the file goes by code, the untag by the URL's folder. Live DB: one patient (881) has two sessions with the same name and date, where even a fresh URL resolves to the lower code. **Fix:** send `tpCode` with the render and the view delete, and resolve by `(person_id, tp_code)` when it is given; take the session's name and date from `timepointsQuery` by code, not the URL; watch and navigate by the 202's `tp_code` | ✅ fixed (RD) |
| FE-F14-4 | [BUG] | 🟡 | `photo-render-watch.ts:75-102` · `PhotoEditor.tsx:336-340` · `photo-editor.routes.ts:218-230,342-367` | **A save that fails at once is reported 105 s later as "taking longer than expected", and a partial failure never says which photo or why.** The watcher is registered after the 202 has been parsed. A render job that finishes within the same instant announces `photos_rendered` before any watcher exists, and the event is dropped. That happens when every slot fails fast: its original was moved or renamed under the open editor, or the slot was malformed. Runtime: *Restore original to re-edit* on tp2 *Profile* → the original moved away on disk → *Save*. Server log: `slot render failed … "Not found"`, `render complete written 0 warnings 1`. In the page, `fetch /render resolved` and `sse photos_rendered` both landed at **4,564 ms**, no outcome toast followed, and at ~105 s the user got "Photo save is taking longer than expected — check the photos grid." No photo was saved, and the screen never said so. When the watcher does catch a partial failure, the toast is a count ("3/4 saved, 1 photo(s) had problems"): the per-slot reasons are collected in `warnings[]` and never leave the server. **Fix:** register before the POST, keyed by a job id the client sends and the server echoes in the event; send the warning texts (view + reason) and show them | ✅ fixed (RD) |
| FE-F14-5 | [BUG] | 🟡 | `RenameFolderModal.tsx:38-41,46-63,70-73` · `SequenceSidebar.tsx:265-273` | ***Rename folder* offers every top-level folder: other sessions' originals and the X-ray folder `OPG` included. Renaming one silently detaches it from its owner.** The dialog lists the patient's folders minus the target and says only "it becomes this timepoint's photo folder". Runtime: the editor for a session with no folder yet (tp4) listed *Final_01-09-2026*, *Initial_01-08-2024*, *Progress_03-01-2026* and *Progress_29-03-2025*, all four of them other sessions' originals. Picking *Initial* renamed it: the folder check for tp0 then returned `exists: false`, and its eight tagged originals now belong to tp4. *Initial* has lost *Restore original* and *Open original folder*, and *Delete everything* on tp4 would now hard-delete them (FE-F12-5's `hardDelete`). Live volume (read-only listing): **1,484** patients have at least one session folder, so a new session's dialog offers another session's originals for every one of them. **3,315** have an `OPG` folder, which the X-ray card reads (`patient-assets.service.ts:158`); renaming it empties that card. The dialog's intended targets look like the **124** folders shaped like `Name_DD-MM-YYYY` that match no session (typos), and in the list they can't be told from the real ones. **Fix:** list only folders that are neither a session's folder nor a reserved one (`OPG`, `OPGIMG`, `CBCT`, …); show the rest apart, with a warning; refuse on the server to rename a folder a session owns unless confirmed | ✅ fixed (RD) |
| FE-F14-6 | [BUG] | 🟡 | `TelegramShareModal.tsx:86-90,294-324` · `queries.ts:1417-1426` · `telegram.routes.ts:42-44,88,146` · `LocalSendShareModal.tsx:112-130,225-231` | **Share progress never ends once the server has dropped the job, and closing a share neither cancels it nor reports how it ended.** (a) **Telegram.** React Query stops `refetchInterval` polling while the tab is hidden, and the server drops a finished job 60 s later. A user who switches away during a minutes-long upload comes back to 404s, and the dialog stays at its last percentage. Runtime: 0 polls in 4 s hidden; then, visible, six 404s in 5 s, and the dialog still read "Sending file 2 of 3 · 40%" with *Sending…* disabled. A restart, or an upload past the 30-min backstop (which also deletes **running** jobs), ends the same way. (b) **LocalSend** treats every failed poll as transient. After a restart: 7 polls in 8 s, 6 of them 404, the header stuck at "Sending to Reception PC…", only *Cancel* offered. (c) **Closing.** ✕, Escape or the backdrop during a LocalSend transfer doesn't cancel it: the receiver's Accept prompt stays live for up to 90 s, and the files go if someone accepts. Neither dialog reports an outcome after it closes, so a user who closes during a Telegram upload can't tell whether to send again, and a second send duplicates the files to the patient. Production logs show 33 Telegram jobs and 89 LocalSend transfers. **Fix:** treat a 404 as final ("Lost track of this send. Check with the recipient"); poll in the background or keep finished jobs longer; report the outcome after close through a module watcher like `photo-render-watch`; cancel on close, or say that closing doesn't cancel | ✅ fixed (RD) |
| FE-F14-7 | [BUG] | 🟡 | `localsend.service.ts:350-364,412-422,627-645` · `localsend.routes.ts:57-64,86-93` · CLAUDE.md *Conventions* | **LocalSend's failures reach the user as raw transport text, not the "is the device on?" message.** CLAUDE.md says the LocalSend client uses `utils/fetch-timeout.ts#describeFetchError`. Both route handlers rely on that in their leak-rule exemption ("its network failures come through … describeFetchError"). The service never imports it. Runtime, the service's own `probe()`: an unreachable address → after **8.0 s** "The operation was aborted."; a closed port → "request to https://127.0.0.1:53317/api/localsend/v2/info failed, reason: connect ECONNREFUSED 127.0.0.1:53317". Both are toasted as-is through *Add by IP*. A transfer whose receiver never taps *Accept* within 90 s, or whose device is asleep, fails with "Failed: The operation was aborted.", which reads as if someone cancelled it. **Fix:** map through `describeFetchError` into copy that names the device and the likely cause ("Reception PC didn't answer within 8 s. Is LocalSend open, and the device on the clinic network?"); correct the CLAUDE.md line in the same change | ✅ fixed (RD) |
| FE-F14-8 | [ENH] | 🔵 | `PhotoEditor.tsx:137-166,478-533` | **The editor hand-rolls its unsaved-work guard and both of its confirm dialogs.** `useBlocker` + its own *Leave photo editor?* modal + its own `beforeunload` + `justSavedRef` re-implement `hooks/useUnsavedRouteGuard` (+ `allowNextNavigation()` before the post-save `navigate`), and *Remove photo?* re-implements `useConfirm`. Both dialogs use bare `<h2>`s instead of `ModalHeader`, and their strings don't come from `common:unsaved.*` (so they won't follow when the editor is translated). The shared guard also leaves same-path navigations alone | ✅ fixed (RD) |
| FE-F14-9 | [BUG] | 🔵 | `SequenceSidebar.tsx:138-139,218-226` · `RenameFolderModal.tsx:54-59` · route table `POST …/files/{upload,folder,rename}` | **The sidebar's upload and rename errors show the funnel's status line** (FE-F12-12's shape): `err.message` gives "Upload failed: HTTP Error: 403 Forbidden" or "Rename failed: HTTP Error: 400 Bad Request", never the server's reason. A doctor or assistant hits the 403 every time: every `photo-editor` route allows `clinical`, but the sidebar's *Upload*, *Move from card* and *Rename folder* call routes gated `admin\|front_desk`. A clinical user can open, frame, save and remove, but cannot bring the originals in. That half is FE-F12-6's owner decision; this row is the message. **Fix:** `httpErrorMessage` | ✅ fixed (RD) |
| FE-F14-10 | [BUG] | 🔵 | `SequenceSidebar.tsx:151-241,256-264` · `PhotoSessionDialog.tsx:279-320` | **On Chrome and Edge, the sidebar's *Upload* button deletes the photos it uploads from the folder they came from.** It is a "move off the memory card": after a 200 it removes each picked file from the remembered *Import folder*. The button says *Upload*. The session dialog calls the folder "Import folder" and never says that files picked from it are deleted. Only the button's tooltip tells. Whatever folder was remembered (a USB stick the doctor wants to keep, a shared camera folder on the PC) loses every file picked from it. The deletion is safe in that it waits for the server's 200 for the whole batch, and multer refuses a whole request rather than skipping a file (clean list), but the user never chose to move. **Fix:** label it *Move from card*, say so in the dialog, confirm on first use, and keep a plain *Upload (copy)* beside it | ✅ fixed (RD) |
| FE-F14-11 | [ENH] | 🔵 | `SlotGrid.tsx:105-110` · `SlotCanvas.tsx:151` · `TelegramShareModal.tsx:56-61,172` | **This clinic's identity and country in the editor and the Telegram dialog** (FE-F12-7's siblings, handed down by F12). The editor's centre cell is `/images/logo.png` with `alt="Shwan Orthodontics"`, and every empty slot shows the same logo. The Telegram dialog keeps its own copy of the contact-account labels, `shw: 'Dr. Shwan Phone'`, instead of reading `shared/google-contacts-accounts.ts` (whose labels are themselves this clinic's, for F16/F22). It also prefixes `'964'` to patient phones in the browser, although the server's formatter already normalises; for a number stored as `07…` the prefix produces `9640750…`, which the server then keeps as-is. Live DB: 5,478 of 5,496 phones are the canonical 10-digit `7…`, so nothing breaks here today. **Fix:** branding logo + `clinicName` alt; labels from the registry; send the stored number and let the server format it | ✅ fixed (RD) |
| FE-F14-12 | [ENH] | 🔵 | `PhotoEditor.tsx:184-197` · `SlotCanvas.tsx:136-147` | **The editor downloads full-resolution renders to fill its read-only slots.** A saved slot is drawn from `/DolImgs/{name}?v=…`, the full render (live: median 2.2 MB, ~18 MP for a facial view; F13's numbers), up to eight per open, to fill cells a few hundred pixels wide. The grid uses the working-files endpoint's 480-px WebP thumbnail for the same cells (`GridComponent.tsx:387-389`). Over the tunnel, opening a saved session downloads ~18 MB before anything is edited. **Fix:** the same thumbnail URL, at 480 px | ✅ fixed (RD) |
| FE-F14-13 | [ENH] | 🔵 | `SlotGrid.tsx:76-83,116-125,157-202` · `SlotContextMenu.tsx:27-67` · `PhotoEditor.tsx:250-281,462-470` · `TelegramShareModal.tsx` · `LocalSendShareModal.tsx` | **Keyboard and screen-reader gaps.** (a) Placing a photo is drag-and-drop only; there is no keyboard or click-to-place path. (b) *Restore original* and *Remove* exist only in the right-click menu. That `role="menu"` gets no focus when it opens and has no arrow keys (FE-F12-13(b)'s shape). (c) The sidebar resizer is a `role="separator"` that can't be focused. (d) Neither share dialog's progress bar has `role="progressbar"`/`aria-valuenow`, and no live region announces "Sent" or "Failed". (e) The Telegram phone field and source `<select>`, and LocalSend's IP and PIN fields, are placeholder-only, with no label. Also: the resizer listens for `pointerup` but not `pointercancel`, so a cancelled touch or pen drag leaves `body` stuck at `cursor: col-resize; user-select: none` until the next pointer-up | ✅ fixed (RD) |
| FE-F14-14 | [BUG] | 🔵 | `PhotoEditor.tsx:176-181` · `SequenceSidebar.tsx:33-38,65,73-76` · `RenameFolderModal.tsx:39` | **Casts over contracted reads** (FE-F12-15's shape). The editor's hydration casts the typed gallery payload to `Array<{ name?: string; mtime?: number } \| null>`, and the folder listing first to `FileListing`, then to a hand-written entry shape. The sidebar keeps a hand-written `FileEntryLite` interface and casts the contract's entries to it. `RenameFolderModal`'s `as FileListing` is a no-op. A server rename of `mtime` or `relPath` would compile and then fail silently at runtime | ✅ fixed (RD) |
| FE-F14-15 | [DEAD] | 🔵 | `PhotoEditor.tsx:1-8` · `photoEditorTypes.ts:37,82,97` | **Leftovers.** The editor's docblock says it is "Mounted (flag-gated) by ContentRenderer. The feature flag is checked there". The flag (`NATIVE_PHOTO_EDITOR_ENABLED`) has no reader left in the tree (`9d24ce4` added it and `d2ce903` removed the check, both 2026-05-27), and the owner's `.env` still sets it. `defaultFlipV`, `OutputDims` and `SlotGridLines` are exported but used only in their own file | ✅ fixed (RD) |

**FE-F14-3 and FE-F14-4 share one fix.** If the render request carries the session code and a client job id, and the server echoes both in its completion event, the watcher can be registered before the request leaves. The save then lands where the screen says, and every outcome is reported, including an instant failure. FE-F14-2 rides on FE-F12-3's single invalidation helper.

**FE-F14-1: how it went unnoticed.** The same sweep converted `TelegramShareModal` and `ShareSheet` to the same pattern, and there it is harmless: their reset only re-seeds state that a fresh mount already has. Only LocalSend's block had a side effect (the scan) that a fresh mount needed. F12 and F13 opened the sheet with LocalSend switched off, where an empty list looks expected.

**Found along the way, not F14's to fix:**
- **`[Telegram] share complete` logs the recipient's full phone number at `info`** (`telegram.routes.ts:82-87`); the production `combined.log` holds them. For F16, with the WhatsApp senders' logging.
- **`GOOGLE_CONTACT_ACCOUNTS` labels are this clinic's** ("Dr. Shwan Phone"), and `SendMessage.tsx:270` hard-codes the same option. For **F16** (send screen) / **F22** (the registry and its Settings card).
- **Clinical in the editor:** see FE-F14-9. It belongs to FE-F12-6's owner decision. If the owner opens patient files to `clinical`, the editor sidebar works for them without further change.

## F13 · Compare · 2026-10-01

Scope read in full: `ComparisonEngine` 672 · `CompareOverlay` 452 · `CompareComponent` 334 · `ControlsPanel` 315 · `CompareStage` 213 · `icons` 176 · `types` 145 · `useCompareShare` 144 · `SelectionPanel` 132 · `geometry` 102 · `useSlideshow` 71 · `useComparisonEngine` 50 — 2,806 LOC — plus their five CSS modules (947 lines: `CompareComponent`, `CompareStage`, `ControlsPanel`, `SelectionPanel`, `CompareOverlay`). Cross-checks the surface forced: `ContentRenderer` and `PatientShell` (how the page mounts, and that nothing keys it by patient), `UniversalHeader`'s last-view navigation, `slideshow/useFullscreen`, `core/http#postFormData` and its default timeout, `query/client.ts`'s defaults, `timepointsQuery`/`timepointImagesQuery`. The contracts: `patient.contract` timepoints / timepointImages / gallery, and `share.contract#stage`. The server halves the page depends on: `timepoint-queries.ts` (the list is ordered by date, and the view list is read from `time_point_images`), `native-timepoint-queries.ts` (a patient's first session is `tp_code` 0), `patient-timepoint.routes.ts` (the three delete scopes, the gallery), `imaging/index.ts#getImageSizes` with `clinic-paths.ts#workingFileNameVariants` (the gallery's two-case probe), `photo-render.service.ts` (renders keep native resolution, up to 8,000 px), the `/DolImgs` mount's cache headers in `app/mount-routes.ts`, and `share.routes.ts` (60 MB multer cap, global 30 s timeout).

**Runtime-verified three ways; nothing was written to the clinic's data or photo volume.** (1) **A throwaway install**, as in F12. PostgreSQL 18 ran from the session scratchpad and was set up with `db:migrate` + `db:setup` ("Bright Smile Orthodontics") + `db:seed:demo`, with `MACHINE_PATH` on an empty ext4 scratch folder and every `.env` key blanked. That volume is case-sensitive, which is how FE-F13-5 showed itself. Upper-case symlinks were then added to stand in for NTFS, so the rest of the page could run. All writes went there: one cropped-only session delete, share-stage uploads, one file "re-rendered" in place, and one real render pair copied in from the clinic volume for real sizes. (2) **The live clinic database and photo volume, read-only**, with the owner's go-ahead given during the session. A dev server ran with the sinks, WhatsApp, Cloudflare and LocalSend off, and Playwright aborted every non-GET (the Share probe measured the upload body, then aborted it). Real patients 5796, 5518, 180 and 5158. (3) **Read-only queries and scans:** SQL on the live DB inside `BEGIN READ ONLY … ROLLBACK`, a scan of `C:\clinic1\working` (names, sizes, pixel dimensions), and the production `combined*.log`. The probe scripts lived in the scratchpad.

### Findings

| ID | Type | Sev | File | One-line | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FE-F13-1 | [BUG] | 🟠 | `CompareComponent.tsx:193-201` · `app/mount-routes.ts:153-163` | **Once a browser has shown a photo in Compare, it keeps showing that version after the photo is re-rendered, and exports it.** Compare builds `/DolImgs/{personId}0{tp}.I{code}` with no version token. The `/DolImgs` mount serves every `.iNN` file with `Cache-Control: private, max-age=31536000, immutable`, on the premise, in its own comment, that "the gallery always requests these with a `?v={mtime}` token". The photo editor re-renders a view under the same name. After a re-crop, the grid shows the new render, while Compare keeps drawing the old one for up to a year in any browser that loaded it before. There is no revalidation, even on a full reload. Runtime: the *Initial*/*Final* Upper pair drawn → `103.i23` re-rendered in place (new content, new mtime; the gallery API reported the new mtime) → full reload, same pair → **the canvas is pixel-identical to before**. A browser with an empty cache drew the new render. The workflow that hits it is Compare's own: see the pair misaligned, fix the crop in the editor, come back. Production log, 9 Jun–21 Sep 2026: **32 of the 438 views rendered were rendered again** (39 extra renders). There is no way out inside the app; only clearing the browser cache helps. **Fix:** build the URLs from the gallery endpoint (`name` + `?v=mtime`), as `GridComponent`, `PhotoEditor` and `PatientSlideshow` do. The same change fixes FE-F13-5 and FE-F13-8. The siblings without a token are in *Found along the way* | ✅ fixed (RD) |
| FE-F13-2 | [BUG] | 🟡 | `CompareComponent.tsx:112-127` · `native-timepoint-queries.ts:55-60` | **For a patient with three or more sessions, Compare opens without the Initial session.** The auto-select takes the first and last session "(skip tp_code 0)". But tp 0 is the patient's **first** session: the allocator starts at `MAX(-1)+1 = 0`. Live DB: **all 1,910** photographed patients have a tp 0, and **1,886** of those are named *Initial*. The **346** with three or more sessions therefore open on *second session vs last*, never *Initial vs Final*. For **at least 85** of them the default pair shares no view at all, so every photo type is disabled until the user re-picks. A re-pick needs an uncheck first, because a third tick toasts "You can only select two timepoints". Runtime, live: 5796 opens on *Progress 20 Jul 2025* / *Progress 13 Sep 2026*, with *Initial 21 Oct 2024* unticked. 5518 opens on *Progress 7 Aug 2024* (3 views) / *Final*, while its *Initial* and *Final* each hold all eight. With exactly two sessions (0 and 1), the fallback branch does pick tp 0. **Fix:** the first and last session in list order (date order), with no `tp_code > 0` filter | ✅ fixed (RD) |
| FE-F13-3 | [BUG] | 🟡 | `CompareComponent.tsx:193` · `SelectionPanel.tsx:31-37` | **"Before" and "After", and which photo goes on top, follow the session code instead of the session date.** Both places sort the two selected codes as numbers. The server orders the list by date on purpose (`timepoint-queries.ts#getTimePoints`: "a backdated timepoint … would then appear out of date order"), and sessions do get backdated and re-dated (*Edit name & date*). Live DB: **25** patients have a code order that differs from their date order. Runtime, live: patient 180 has sessions 0 *Initial* (26 Jan 2017), 2 *Progress* (25 Feb 2017) and 1 *Progress* (25 Mar 2017). It opens with its default pair **reversed**: 25 Mar is badged *Before*, 25 Feb *After*, and the March photo is drawn on top. On the demo patient, with the list reordered so tp 1 comes after *Final*, *Retention* (Dec 2026) is badged *Before* and drawn first (`101.I20`, then `103.I20`). The exported picture carries no labels, so it reads as the treatment in reverse. **Fix:** order the pair by `(tp_date_time, tp_code)` from the list, in both places | ✅ fixed (RD) |
| FE-F13-4 | [BUG] | 🟡 | `CompareComponent.tsx:171-209,239-253` · `ComparisonEngine.ts:165-185` · `CompareStage.tsx:91,190` · `useCompareShare.ts:52-56,116-118` | **The canvas can show a pair other than the one selected, and *Save*/*Share* export it.** The engine keeps its last image set until a new one has loaded, and nothing clears it. (a) **A failed load.** `loadImages` throws on the first 404, error or timeout, and the previous pair stays drawn while the panel shows the new type and *Save* stays enabled. Runtime: *Right* pair drawn → *Center* chosen, with `.I22` made to fail → toast; panel on *Center*; canvas unchanged → *Save* downloaded `comparison_1_2026-10-01.png` at 960×1038, which is the **Right** pair. The loads that fail in practice are FE-F13-5, FE-F13-8 and FE-F13-9's timeout. (b) **An incomplete selection.** Untick one session and the old pair stays on screen. The empty state ("Select one more timepoint") stays hidden because it keys on `imageCount >= 2`, and the stepper reads `1 Timepoints · 2 Photo type · ✓ Compare & export` (runtime). Ticking a new pair before choosing a type does the same. **Fix:** give the engine a `clear()` and call it whenever the selection stops being complete; mark the drawn set stale on a failed load; enable *Save*/*Share* only while the drawn URLs match the selection | ✅ fixed (RD) |
| FE-F13-5 | [BUG] | 🟡 | `CompareComponent.tsx:194` · `imaging/index.ts:56-62` · `clinic-paths.ts:94-102` | **Compare asks for `.I10` in upper case, and native renders are saved as `.i10`.** NTFS ignores the difference. A case-sensitive volume doesn't, and a Linux server is on the plan (CLAUDE.md, *Deployment*). On one, every photo rendered by the app's own editor 404s in Compare. Runtime, on the throwaway's ext4 volume: `/DolImgs/101.I10` → **404**, `/DolImgs/101.i10` → 200. The page toasts "Failed to load comparison images: Failed to load image: /DolImgs/101.I10" and never draws. The gallery already handles this: `getImageSizes` probes `workingFileNameVariants` (lower case first, then the Dolphin-era upper case) and returns the name that resolved. The live volume holds both: **1,058 of the 1,068** renders written since June are lower case, and **21,658** view files exist only in upper case (Dolphin era). No single fixed case works everywhere, and a new deployment has no Dolphin files, so every Compare load there 404s. **Fix:** FE-F13-1's (take `name` from the gallery) | ✅ fixed (RD) |
| FE-F13-6 | [BUG] | 🟡 | `ComparisonEngine.ts:408-410,414-447` · `useCompareShare.ts:52-56,77,116-128` · `share.routes.ts:26-29` | **The export is a full-resolution PNG, so *Share* times out off the LAN, and phones have to hold canvases of up to 36 MP.** *Auto (100%)*, the default, sizes the canvas to twice the larger render, and renders keep their native resolution (up to 8,000 px). Live volume, recent renders: facial views are a median of ~18 MP each (max 30.5 MP), intraoral ~11 MP. Measured in the page, live patients 5796 and 5158: canvases of **25.7–36.0 MP** and PNGs of **29.5–45.1 MB**, with each `toDataURL` blocking the main thread for **0.95–1.4 s** (on this desktop). *Save* took 4–5 s for 45 MB. On a desktop, *Share* uploads the whole PNG through `postFormData` on the default 30 s timeout (FE-F1-2's shape; RA1 raised it only for videos and patient files). At LAN speed it staged in 0.2 s. At a 10 Mbit/s upload (off the LAN, through the tunnel) it failed at +30 s with **"Request timed out after 30000ms"** (runtime, real-size pair). A horizontal pair of the largest facial renders would also exceed the 60 MB stage cap. On a phone, a 36 MP canvas plus its two decoded images is about 300 MB. iOS Safari caps a canvas at about 16.7 MP, below every real pair. That, and whether a phone can encode inside native share's 5 s user-activation window, were **not measured on a device**. **Fix:** encode with `canvas.toBlob('image/jpeg', 0.9)` (async, and a fraction of the size); cap *Auto* at a long edge (e.g. 4,096 px), keeping full size as an explicit option; give the stage upload the upload timeout | ✅ fixed (RD) |
| FE-F13-7 | [ENH] | 🟡 | `compare/types.ts:126-128` · `ComparisonEngine.ts:92,236-238` · `ControlsPanel.tsx:256-275` | **Every exported comparison carries this clinic's logo by default** (CLAUDE.md's identity rule; handed down by FE-F12-7). `LOGO_WHITE_URL`/`LOGO_BLACK_URL` are `public/images/logo_white.png` and `logo.png`, this clinic's wordmark ("… ORTHODONTICS"), and `showLogo` starts `true`. These are the images that leave the building: to patients, by Telegram, and through the *Post*/*Story* social-media presets. Runtime, on the Bright Smile install: every pair load requests `/images/logo_white.png`, and the logo is drawn at 15 % of the canvas width. A configurable logo already exists (`brandingQuery().logo`, `CLINIC_LOGO`), but it is one image and the colour toggle needs two. **Fix:** watermark with the branding logo when one is set; when none is, start with the watermark off and hide both toggles. Make the white/black pair an optional second upload, or derive it with a canvas filter | ✅ fixed (RD) |

Live scan: **20** rows point at a missing file (5 sessions, 3 patients), and **35** view files have no row. **Fix:** FE-F13-1's (the gallery reflects the files) | ✅ fixed (RD) |
| FE-F13-9 | [BUG] | 🔵 | `ComparisonEngine.ts:165-202` | **Images load one at a time, each has a hard 10 s limit, and nothing is ever cancelled.** (a) The three images (two photos, then the logo) load in sequence: photo 2 is requested only once photo 1 is done (runtime). (b) Each image is rejected at 10 s while its download carries on. Runtime, the real-size pair at a 2.5 Mbit/s download (a phone on a weak link): the 3.5 MB render finished at **11.4 s**, but the load had failed at 10 s with "Timeout loading image: /DolImgs/102.I24", and nothing was drawn. The median live render is 2.2 MB (p90 4.4 MB). (c) A superseded load keeps fetching. Runtime: four fast *next* presses in the slideshow, with images delayed 0.8 s, fetched **10** photos for the 5 slides passed. **Fix:** load in parallel under one `AbortController` per `loadImages` call, abort it when superseded, and let that replace the fixed timeout | ✅ fixed (RD) |
| FE-F13-10 | [BUG] | 🔵 | `ComparisonEngine.ts:165-185` · `useSlideshow.ts:36-51` | **Position, zoom, rotation and crop set for one photo type carry over to the next.** `loadImages` keeps the transforms and crops; only the slideshow calls `reset()` on each step. A different view with the previous view's alignment is never what is wanted. **Fix:** reset on a photo-type change. Whether to keep the alignment across a change of sessions for the same view is a product call | ✅ fixed (RD) |

Runtime, live patient 5158 in `America/New_York`: *Initial 11/20/2023* and *Final 9/20/2026*, against 11/21 and 9/21 at +3. It is also one of FE-F3-3's browser-locale sites | ✅ fixed (RD) |

It becomes live on the day any link goes from one patient's Compare to another's. **Fix, one line, to ride along with the first RD fix:** `key={personId}` on the page, plus FE-F13-4's `clear()` | ✅ fixed (RD) |

Dragging the photo itself works: in the runtime check, a one-finger drag selected Image 1 and moved it without scrolling the page (`touch-action: none` holds). There is no pinch zoom; the wheel is the only zoom gesture. (c) By reading: iPhone Safari has no element fullscreen, so `enter()` swallows the TypeError, the Fullscreen button does nothing, and the slideshow runs inline with no overlay fallback | ✅ fixed (RD) |
