| FE-F5-19 | [BUG] | 🟡 | `aligner-portal-external/src/lib/api.ts:476` (portal repo) | **cross-scope (portal):** `fetchAnnouncements` filters `expires_at.gt.<new Date().toISOString()>` — a UTC string against a clinic wall-clock column — so **every** expiring announcement (all 125 today) stays on doctors' screens **3 h past its expiry** | ✅ fixed 2026-09-28 — RLS applied to the mirror, portal `3977cbe` live |
| FE-F5-20 | [BUG] | 🔵 | `aligner-portal-external/functions/api/cases/[[path]].ts:103-117,244` + its Edge twin (portal repo) | **cross-scope (portal):** the new-case function computes "today" with local getters in Cloudflare/Deno, which run in **UTC** — a case submitted 00:00–03:00 clinic time gets the previous day as its set `creation_date`, provenance-note date and DOB anchor | ✅ fixed 2026-09-28 — Pages env set, portal `3977cbe` live, verified in production |

**FE-F5-1 — the mirror's clock runs in UTC, and last-write-wins trusts it.**
*Found through the bell, but the defect is in the sync layer — filed here so it is not lost, fixed by whoever owns `services/sync/` and the Supabase DDL, not by an RB session.* Both databases stamp times with `LOCALTIMESTAMP`, which evaluates in each server's own `TimeZone`: local `shwan` is `Asia/Baghdad`, the Supabase mirror is **`UTC`** (no database-level or role-level override — checked `pg_db_role_setting` and `pg_roles.rolconfig`; `SELECT localtimestamp` read `15:22` on the mirror against `18:22` local at the same instant). A row the portal writes directly on the mirror therefore carries a **UTC wall-clock** value in a `timestamp without time zone` column, and the reverse sink copies it home verbatim (that is its keystone — `set_updated_at()` skips under `app.cdc_origin='reverse'`), where every reader takes it for Baghdad wall-clock. `migrations/supabase/expenses-created-at-2026-09-07.sql` already names the asymmetry and dismisses it as "only ever reached by a row INSERTed directly on the mirror" — which is precisely what the doctor portal does. Two consequences:
- **Displayed times (the F5 symptom).** `aligner_activity_flags.created_at` defaults to `LOCALTIMESTAMP` and the portal's `tryCreateActivityFlag` omits it, so a portal note posted a minute ago reads **"3h"** in the Portal-activity bell, and events between 00:00 and 03:00 clinic time are grouped under the previous day (`dayKey`). The same skew reaches every portal-authored timestamp shown on the staff side — `aligner_notes`, `doctor_announcement_reads` (F17/F18 surfaces).
- **Lost writes (why this is 🟠).** `set_updated_at_remote()` stamps `NEW.updated_at := localtimestamp` on every genuine mirror edit, and `lwwUpdateClause` applies a reverse change only `WHERE EXCLUDED.updated_at > local.updated_at`. So a portal UPDATE made up to **3 h after** a staff edit of the same row compares as *older* and is silently skipped on the local side, while the mirror — and therefore the portal, which reads from it — shows it as saved. Concrete path: staff mark batch 123 delivered at 10:00 (local `updated_at` 10:00, forwarded); the doctor changes wear-days 14 → 10 on the portal at 11:30 (`updateBatchDays`, mirror `updated_at` := 08:30); reverse apply `08:30 > 10:00` is false → local keeps 14, the portal says 10. The five aligner tables the portal writes or creates rows in all carry the trigger (`aligner_batches`, `aligner_notes`, `aligner_activity_flags`, `doctor_announcement_reads`, `aligner_sets` — checked `pg_trigger` on the mirror). **Not 🔴 only because nothing has happened yet:** all 492 `aligner_batches` rows agree on `days` and `updated_at` across both sides today, and inserts (most portal traffic) do not go through the LWW comparison. On a deployment west of UTC the bias inverts and it is *staff* edits that lose. Fix is the owner's: make the mirror stamp clinic wall-clock (per-deployment `ALTER DATABASE … SET timezone`, or `now() AT TIME ZONE <clinic tz>` in the remote trigger and defaults) and decide what to do about the rows already stamped in UTC.

*Owner decision, 2026-09-27: **option A** (set the mirror's database timezone — one line, fixes all 24 defaults and both remote triggers, keeps DDL parity; the trigger-rewrite, sink-side conversion and `timestamptz` options were rejected), **leave the already-stamped rows** (bad `updated_at` values are only ever too low, so the first new write on either side supersedes them; the ~38 display times were all ≥ 3 days old), and **add a guard**.* Done the same day, gate green (216/216 tests):
- **`services/sync/cdc/clock-check.ts`** (+ pure `clock-compare.ts`, 8 unit tests) — on every install, a minute after boot and every 6 h, compares the app server's `TZ`, local PostgreSQL's and the mirror's **database-level** zone **by UTC offset**, logs `[cdc:clock] CLOCKS DISAGREE — …` naming the exact fix, and reports `clock` on `GET /api/sync/supabase-status`, rendered as a banner on Settings → Supabase status. Each database is judged against the **app server's** zone, so an install where local is the misconfigured one is never told to copy local's zone onto the mirror. Runtime-verified against the live (still UTC) mirror: log line, endpoint and banner all report the 3 h mismatch.
- **`migrations/supabase/mirror-timezone-2026-09-27.sql`** — the change plus its apply/recycle/verify/rollback steps; the invariant is now in `docs/sync-cdc.md` (§Don't regress, "One clock on both sides") and CLAUDE.md's Sync section.
- **Applied 2026-09-27, ~23:36 clinic time** (reverse backlog 0): `ALTER DATABASE postgres SET timezone TO 'Asia/Baghdad'` on the mirror (`pg_db_role_setting` confirms it; no role-level `timezone` shadows it). A new psql session right afterwards **still read UTC**, because `.env` reaches the mirror through **Supavisor's session pooler (:5432)**, which reuses server backends across client sessions. So a service restart alone would not have picked it up. The idle `postgres`/`Supavisor` backend and the portal's `authenticator`/`postgrest` one (open since 2026-08-28) were terminated, and so was the backend doing the terminating, so the pooler could not hand it back. Afterwards the mirror and local both read `Asia/Baghdad` at the same wall-clock minute, and PostgREST and the app pool reconnected on their own with new backends. Only Supabase's internal workers (`pg_cron`, `pg_net`, exporter, auth_query) predate the change; none of them stamp app rows. The runbook's step 2 now covers the pooler. The two **portal-side** clock bugs no database setting reaches were filed separately: **FE-F5-19** (announcement expiry filtered against a UTC string) and **FE-F5-20** (new-case "today" computed in Cloudflare's UTC). **Both were fixed and deployed on 2026-09-28** — see their entries below.

**FE-F5-2 — an approval lands, the screen behind the bell does not move.**
`runAction` (`:114`) does `notifyApprovalsChanged()` (which only makes the bells refetch their own lists) and `invalidateQueries(qk.approvals.mine())` — a key that only `MyApprovalsBadge` observes, and that badge is never rendered for an admin. Nothing else is invalidated, although `approve()` runs the held write for real: `approval-actions.ts` applies `work.update`/`work.discount`/`work.delete`/`invoice.delete`/`expense.update`/`expense.delete`/`patient.delete`. The bell's own patient chip navigates to `/patient/:id/works` "to review", so review-then-approve is the designed flow. Runtime: with `/patient/7639/works` mounted, clicking ✓ on a (mocked) `invoice.delete` hold produced exactly two GETs — `/api/csrf-token` and `/api/approvals?status=pending` — and no works/patient refetch — so after a real approval the card would go on showing the deleted invoice and the old balance until the admin navigates (the probe fulfilled the POST locally; nothing was deleted). Fix: on approve/approve-all, invalidate `qk.patient.all(row.person_id)` + `qk.work.all()` for the work/invoice/patient actions and `qk.expenses.all()` for the expense ones (or `qk.approvals.all()` + those, once FE-F5-13 retires the event bus). Approve-all needs the broad form, since its rows span patients.

**FE-F5-3 — a failed approval looks exactly like a successful one.**
`approve()` has five outcomes, and `routes/api/approval.routes.ts` maps three of them — `missing`, `stale` ("Target changed since request was submitted") and `failed` ("Could not apply: …") — to **`sendData(…, result.row)` with status 200**, carrying `status: 'stale' | 'failed'` in the row and the reason in the envelope message. `approveRequest()` returns that row; `handleApprove` discards it. So the promise resolves, no toast fires, `notifyApprovalsChanged()` refetches, and the row disappears from the bell — the identical sequence to a real approval. Failure scenario: front desk submits a discount on a work, someone edits the same work before the admin gets to it, the admin clicks ✓ — the discount was **not** applied, and the admin has no way to know (the only trace is the submitter's badge flipping to "Stale" up to 5 minutes later, and there is no history screen — FE-F5-16). `handleApproveAll` already reads its result and says "N skipped"; the single path needs the same: read `row.status` and toast `review_note` when it is not `approved`. (Also here: no in-flight guard, so a double-click sends a second approve that correctly 409s — "This approval was already processed" — and pops an error after a success.)

**FE-F5-4 — typing a snooze date snoozes on the first digit of the year.**
The quick-snooze row's `<input type="date" onChange={(e) => e.target.value && handleSnooze(t, e.target.value)} />` commits on every change event, and Chromium emits one as soon as all three segments hold *something*. Runtime, keystroke by keystroke: `1`,`0` (month) → `''`; `1`,`5` (day) → `''`; `2` (year) → `PUT /api/alerts/999001/snooze {"snoozedUntil":"0002-10-15"}`, and the row closed. `0002-10-15` passes `alertSnooze`'s `dateString` (regex + real calendar date), the server stores it, and because it is in the past the task stays in the bell as though the snooze had failed. There is no `min` either, so a past date picked from the calendar popup does the same. Only the popup path, used carefully, works. Fix: commit on blur/Enter (or a small Apply button), add `min={tomorrow}`.

**FE-F5-5 — the edit form's "None" and "Anyone" are not what they say.**
(a) `handleSave` sends `alertTypeId: alertTypeId ? parseInt(…) : undefined`; JSON drops `undefined`, `alertBody.alertTypeId` is `intId.optional()` (no null), and `updateAlert` only writes the keys it is given — so choosing **None** on a categorised task keeps the category and toasts "Task updated". Runtime body after selecting None on a task in category 1: `{"alertSeverity":2,"alertDetails":"E2E mock task","surfaceMode":"push","expiresAt":"","assignedTo":null}`. The fix needs the contract to accept `null` (as `assignedTo` already does), so it touches `shared/contracts/patient.contract.ts`. (b) The staff picker is `employeesQuery()` = active employees only, while a task assigned to someone who has since quit keeps that assignment (deliberately — `getAlertAssignedTo` exists to let such a task be re-saved). Editing one, the controlled `<select value="7">` has no option 7, so the browser shows **"Anyone (unassigned)"** while state still holds 7; Save preserves the quit assignee the form says is not there, and picking "Anyone" fires no change event because it is already displayed. 0 such rows today (both employees who quit have no tasks) — latent half, shipped half.

**FE-F5-6 — the patient's alert list is one of the task surfaces, and no task write refreshes it.**
`getAlertsByPersonId` returns every *active, unexpired* alert for the patient with **no `surface_mode` filter**, so a push task linked to a patient is also a row in `ViewPatientInfo`'s alert list (`qk.patient.alerts(id)`). Every task write on the F5 surface leaves that list stale: bell Done/Dismiss/Edit, `TaskFormModal` create-with-patient, `TasksHistory` Done/Dismiss/Reopen/Delete. Nothing listens for `tasks:changed` except `TasksBell` itself (grep: one `addEventListener(TASKS_CHANGED_EVENT)`), even though `services/tasks.ts:6` and `TasksHistory.tsx:84` both say the patient view does. Scenario: front desk has the patient's info page open, dismisses that patient's escalated alert from the bell, and the alert is still listed on the page underneath. Fix: each write invalidates `qk.patient.alerts(person_id)` when the row has one (the reverse direction is already right — `ViewPatientInfo`'s own dismiss calls `notifyTasksChanged()`).

**FE-F5-7 — the history page does not know tasks expire.** `getHeaderTasks` hides a task once `expires_at < CURRENT_DATE`; `getAllTasks` does not filter it, and `displayStatus()` only distinguishes snoozed from active. So an expired task reads **Active**, offers Done/Dismiss, and "Reopen" on a finished task whose expiry has passed toasts "Task reopened" for a row that can never reappear in the bell. Add an `expired` display state (and don't offer Reopen, or clear `expires_at` when reopening). Two smaller things on the same page: the "full task log" is the 200 most recent push tasks with **client-side** search, so an older task yields "No matches" rather than "not in the last 200", and the route's `RouteErrorBoundary` is still named "Completed Tasks" from before the page became the full log.

**FE-F5-8 — the date-range tabs undo their factory's guard.** `revenueBreakdownQuery`/`doctorCommissionsQuery` both declare `enabled: !!startDate && !!endDate`; both views spread them and then write `enabled: !invalidRange`, which **replaces** the factory's value, and `invalidRange` is false when a date is empty. Clearing the From field (the date input's clear, or Backspace on a segment) fires `GET /api/statistics/revenue-breakdown?startDate=&endDate=2026-09-30` and the tab shows the server's **"Invalid request query"** — runtime-confirmed. `PeriodNavigator.stepMonth` with an empty start splits `''` into `[0]` and emits `NaN-NaN-NaN`. And like FE-F5-4, each typed year digit is a committed range (`0002-…`), which here only costs a pointless aggregate query per keystroke. Fix: `enabled: !invalidRange && !!startDate && !!endDate` (or compose with the factory's), and guard `stepMonth`.

**FE-F5-9 — two year inputs, both broken, in opposite directions.** (a) The month-nav year is `setYear(parseInt(value, 10))` on every keystroke: typing 2025 over 2026 fired `/api/statistics?month=9&year=2`, `…=20`, `…=202`, `…=2025` (runtime); clearing it gives `NaN`, which the URL effect writes into `?year=NaN` and the server rejects with a 400 the page then shows instead of the data. (b) The Yearly view's From/To inputs accept a value only when it is already in `[2000, other end]`, and because they are controlled, every rejected keystroke is reverted — selecting "2022" and typing 2019 leaves **2022** (runtime). Only the spinner arrows can change them. Keep a draft string and commit on blur/Enter, validating once.

**FE-F5-10 — the statistics payload is re-typed by assertion.** `(statisticsData ?? null) as StatisticsData | null` (and the same for `YearlyData`, `MultiYearData`) over reads that `statisticsQuery` has already parsed with `reports.statistics.response`. The hand-written interfaces disagree with the contract in both directions: they add `success: boolean` and `error?: string` (not in the payload — `core/http` unwraps the envelope), declare `Day: string` where the contract has `Day` optional (`openDayModal`'s `if (!dayStr) return` shows the author knew), and omit `summary.cashBox`. The sharp edge is `exchangeRate`: the page's money notice branches on `statistics.exchangeRate != null`, and `exchangeRate` exists only in the loose container — it is **not modeled** — so if the server ever drops or renames it the page silently switches to "No exchange rate has been recorded yet … the Grand Total columns show —" on a month that has one, and the contract guard cannot fire. Model it (`exchangeRate: z.number().nullable()`) and delete the three interfaces. Same file: `const isAdmin = user?.role === 'admin'` next to `roleCaps(...)` — the registry's doc says "never re-derive ad hoc `isAdmin` booleans"; the admin-only report tier deserves a capability (`viewReports`) the way `viewFinance` covers the rest.

**FE-F5-11 — "restore the last date" restores yesterday.** `DailyAppointments` writes `sessionStorage['lastAppointmentDate']` on every date change; the Dashboard's Appointments card and `UniversalHeader`'s Appointments tab both navigate to it if present. `sessionStorage` lives as long as the tab and **survives reloads**, and a reception PC's tab is not closed overnight — so the first click of the day opens yesterday's list, which, with FE-F3-9, is also still labelled Live. Restore only a date saved today (store `{date, savedOn}`), or only a date that is not in the past. The two readers are copies of each other, each with its own hand-rolled "today" builder (FE-F1-8's census).

**FE-F5-12 — the bells are a mouse-only surface.** Each popover is `createPortal(<div role="dialog">…, document.body)` — appended to the end of the document — and opening one moves no focus, sets no `aria-haspopup`/`aria-controls`, and adds no Tab path in. Runtime: with the Tasks popover open, Tab from the bell landed on the **Portal activity** button (`insidePopover: false`). A keyboard user can reach Approve/Reject/Done/Snooze/Edit/Mark-read only by tabbing through the whole page. Two Escape papercuts in the same shell: the popover's `document` keydown listener is not part of RA2's modal stack, so with **Approve all**'s confirm open, one Escape closed both the confirm and the popover (runtime: 2 surfaces → 0); and Escape inside the reject-reason input cancels the reject *and* closes the popover. Fix belongs in one shared popover (FE-F5-13): focus the first item on open, return focus to the bell on close, and ignore Escape while a modal is on the stack.

**FE-F5-13 — four bells, one component, four copies.** `placePopover` + the outside-click/Escape/resize effect + the visibility-and-event refetch effect are the same ~50 lines in all four; `relAge` is copied four times, `ACTION_LABELS` twice, and `REFRESH_MS` is declared in `TasksBell`, `services/approvals.ts` and (as `PORTAL_ACTIVITY_REFRESH_MS`) `services/portal-activity.ts`. The visibility handler calls `refetch()` unconditionally, so every return to the tab costs an admin three GETs regardless of freshness — RQ's per-query `refetchOnWindowFocus` would respect `staleTime`. `(data ?? []) as TaskRow[]`/`as ApprovalRow[]` cast data that is already exactly that type. **FE-F1-10 verdict (F2 left it open): retire the buses.** Every listener is a component that already holds an RQ query, so `invalidateQueries({ queryKey: qk.tasks.all() })` (or `qk.approvals.all()`, `qk.portalActivity.all()`) reaches it with no event; the fourteen `notify*Changed()` call sites (5 tasks · 8 approvals · 1 portal activity) become invalidations, and FE-F5-2/FE-F5-6 then read as "invalidate the right keys", which is the house idiom.

**FE-F5-14 — one translated bell in a translated header.** `RootLayout` pins the header's `dir` to the language because the header is "persistent, always-translated chrome", and `PortalActivityBell` is on the i18n ratchet (`common:portalActivity.*`). Its three siblings are not: in an Arabic session the header reads Arabic, then "Tasks · Nothing waiting on you · Mark done · Snooze", "Approvals · Holds · Approve all", "My submissions · Pending", and `TaskFormModal` opens entirely English. Separately, `AlertModal` ("Add New Alert", "Severity Level", "Save Alert", every toast) is opened from the translated `ViewPatientInfo` — FE-F3-13's shape: the ratchet checks files, and the child modal is not on the list.

**FE-F5-15 — the chart and the dates.** (a) The trend chart themes its ticks and grid through `getChartThemeColors()` but sets no `plugins.title.color`, so the title renders in Chart.js's default `#666` on the dark card. (b) Day labels, `formatDate` and `aggregateByMonth` all do `new Date('YYYY-MM-DD')` — which parses as **UTC midnight** — and then read it with local getters, so on any negative-offset host every label is the previous day and a month's first day is aggregated into the prior month. Correct today only because the clinic is `+03`; `utils/calendarDate.ts#parseLocalDate` is the existing fix (the backend audit fixed this same shape in `aligner.routes.ts`). (c) Refresh calls only `refetchStatistics`, so in the Monthly/Yearly views it does not refresh the chart's rollup data. (d) `?view=` is read once, cast `as ViewMode` without validation, and never written back, so a tab switch is lost on reload or Back while `?month=/year=/day=` all round-trip.

**FE-F5-16 — the approvals audit trail: build it.** F2 found `GET /api/approvals/history` (admin, 500 rows, contracted) with a client factory and no consumer, and left the call to F5. Having read the bell: once an admin approves, rejects or acknowledges, the row leaves the bell and **nothing** in the UI shows the decision again — not to the admin, and the requester sees only their own via `MyApprovalsBadge`. With FE-F5-3 that means a stale/failed approval is invisible to the one person who needs to know. Everything below the screen already exists; a `/approvals/history` page cloned from `/tasks/history` plus a header icon in `ApprovalsBell` (the "All tasks" button pattern in `TasksBell`) is small. Owner call — if the answer is retire, delete the route, the contract entry, `approvalsHistoryQuery` and `qk.approvals.history`.

**FE-F5-17 — Dashboard small change.** `isRtl` is read from `document.documentElement.dir` during render; the `dir` is applied by `RootLayout`'s effect *after* the language-change render, so in customize mode the first arrow click after a language flip moves the card the wrong way (it self-heals on the next render). `cardMap.get(key as any)` is F0's lone `as any` here (type `savedOrder` as the card-key union instead). `isVisibleCard` returns true while `user?.role` is unknown, so a clinical user sees the Statistics/Stand cards for a beat on a cold load. `.customizeButtonActive` carries two `!important`s that are neither print nor a11y (F24's rule).

**FE-F5-18 — a count that isn't a total.** `DoctorPaymentsModal`'s header strip shows Collected IQD, Collected USD (from the clicked aggregate row — whole period) and **Payments** = `rows.length`. When the server truncates, the note directly below says "Showing the N most recent payments only … The totals above cover the whole period", but "Payments" is the truncated count. Label it "Listed" or suppress it when `truncated`. F8 owns the file.

**FE-F5-19 — the portal shows expired announcements for three more hours.** *(Portal repo — `aligner-portal-external/`, its own CLAUDE.md and deploy. Split out of FE-F5-1 on 2026-09-27; fixed in code the same night — see **Done** below.)* `fetchAnnouncements(drId)` (`src/lib/api.ts:468-482`) reads `doctor_announcements` with `.or(\`expires_at.is.null,expires_at.gt.${new Date().toISOString()}\`)`. `expires_at` is `timestamp WITHOUT time zone` written by the staff app in clinic wall-clock; the filter value is a UTC ISO string, and Postgres **ignores the `Z`** when casting to `timestamp without time zone`, so the comparison is clinic-wall-clock expiry vs UTC-wall-clock now — every announcement stays visible 3 h after it expired (live DB: 125 announcements, all with an expiry time). It also trusts the doctor's device clock. **The mirror `ALTER DATABASE … SET timezone` (FE-F5-1) does not fix this** — the value is computed in the browser. Fix, preferred: move the predicate server-side, where the mirror's own clock (clinic zone once FE-F5-1's ALTER is applied) decides — add `AND (expires_at IS NULL OR expires_at > LOCALTIMESTAMP)` to the doctors' SELECT policy on `doctor_announcements` in a new `sql/` file next to `sql/phase3-announcements.sql` (check the `doctor_announcement_reads` INSERT policy's "announcement visible to them" subquery — dismissing an expired one then 42501s, which `dismissAnnouncement` already treats as success), then drop the client `.or(expires_at…)`. **Order matters:** apply the SQL (operator, via the Supabase SQL editor / `SUPABASE_FAILOVER_DB_URL`) and the FE-F5-1 ALTER **before** deploying a client that no longer filters, or expired announcements would show indefinitely. Alternative if DDL is unwanted: compute a clinic-local `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:mm:ss` (no `Z`) with `Intl.DateTimeFormat(…, { timeZone })` from a new `VITE_CLINIC_TIMEZONE` build var — per-deployment config, never a literal. Verify: an announcement whose `expires_at` is a few minutes past disappears from `AnnouncementBanner` immediately, not 3 h later. Update the portal CLAUDE.md's `fetchAnnouncements` row and the SQL-file list.

*Done 2026-09-27 (preferred fix):* **`sql/phase3b-announcement-expiry.sql`** recreates `portal_select` on `doctor_announcements` with `and (expires_at is null or expires_at > localtimestamp)` — no zone named, so it rides the mirror-clock invariant; `sql/phase3-announcements.sql` carries the same policy text so a re-run cannot regress it. `fetchAnnouncements` no longer filters expiry, and its doc comment says why. The reads INSERT policy was left as it is: RLS applies inside its "visible to them" subquery, so a dismiss of a since-expired announcement 42501s, which `dismissAnnouncement` already swallows (comment updated). Portal CLAUDE.md: SQL-file list, the announcements section (new "expiry is enforced by RLS, never in the browser" rule) and the `fetchAnnouncements` row. Portal `tsc` + `vite build` clean. Pre-apply read on the mirror: `TimeZone` = `Asia/Baghdad`; live `portal_select` still has no expiry predicate; 125 announcements, all with an expiry, 85 already expired. **Step 1 done, 2026-09-27 ~23:55 clinic time:** the SQL was applied to the mirror with `scripts/psql.sh supa -f …`. The live `portal_select` now reads `(target … ) AND ((expires_at IS NULL) OR (expires_at > LOCALTIMESTAMP))`. Run as `authenticated` with `dr_id` 1 in a rolled-back transaction, doctor 1 sees exactly their 20 unexpired announcements, with 0 expired and 0 belonging to another doctor. Evaluated as that doctor, the reads-INSERT visibility subquery admits a live announcement (103) and refuses an expired one (12); this was checked side-effect-free, so no receipt was inserted and no sequence value was used. The old client keeps working unchanged, since its filter and the policy AND together. **Step 2 done, 2026-09-28:** portal `3977cbe` pushed. Pages deployment `4c923e9d` succeeded, and its served bundle no longer contains the `expires_at.gt` filter; the only `expires_at` references left are supabase-js's own session handling.

**FE-F5-20 — a case submitted after midnight is dated yesterday.** *(Portal repo; split out of FE-F5-1; fixed in code the same night — see **Done** below.)* `functions/api/cases/[[path]].ts` builds `today = localDateStr(new Date())` (`:244`) and `approxDobFromAge()` (`:114-118`) with `getFullYear/getMonth/getDate` — "local" getters that on Cloudflare Pages Functions (and Deno Edge) are **UTC**. `today` becomes `aligner_sets.creation_date` (a `date`, no DB default), the provenance note's "Created via doctor portal … on <date>", and the anchor for the approximate DOB. For a submission between 00:00 and 03:00 clinic time all three are the previous day (none of the 4 portal cases so far fell in that window, so no existing row is wrong). **Must change in lockstep in BOTH twins** — `functions/api/cases/[[path]].ts` (production) and `supabase/functions/aligner-portal-cases/index.ts` (the smoke-test target; its header says any change must be mirrored). Fix: derive the date in the clinic zone — `new Intl.DateTimeFormat('en-CA', { timeZone: env.PORTAL_CLINIC_TIMEZONE, year: 'numeric', month: '2-digit', day: '2-digit' }).format(now)` — with the zone as a new **required** knob next to `PORTAL_CASE_WORK_TYPE_ID/_DR_ID/_CURRENCY` (fail 500 loudly if unset, same as those; validate it by constructing the formatter in a try), set on **both** Cloudflare Pages and the Supabase function, documented in `scripts/deploy-cases-fn.ps1`'s header and the portal CLAUDE.md's env list. (Considered and not preferred: `DEFAULT CURRENT_DATE` on `aligner_sets.creation_date` — a schema change on both DBs, and it still leaves the note date and DOB in JS.) Verify with `node scripts/test-cases-fn.mjs` against the Edge twin, then a Pages redeploy.

*Done 2026-09-27 (preferred fix), in both twins identically:* `localDateStr`/`approxDobFromAge()` replaced by `clinicDateFormat(tz)` (an `Intl.DateTimeFormat` for the zone, `null` when unset or not a valid IANA name, so it fails 500 like the other `PORTAL_CASE_*` knobs), `clinicDateStr(fmt, now)` (via `formatToParts`, not a locale's output format) and `approxDobFromAge(today, age)`, which does its arithmetic on the clinic `today` string through `Date.UTC`, so the DOB is anchored on the same date as the set and the note. The zone knob is **`PORTAL_CLINIC_TIMEZONE`**. `deploy-cases-fn.ps1` sets it as a Supabase secret, reading it from the main `.env` and falling back to `TZ`, and its header and closing reminder now name it for the Pages env as well. `test-cases-fn.mjs` now asserts `creation_date`, the note's date and the DOB against the clinic's today. Helper check (process pinned to `TZ=UTC`, like Workers/Deno): 00:30, 02:59 and 03:00 clinic time all date to the clinic day, while the old code gave the previous day at 00:30; the leap day maps to Mar-1; the year rolls over correctly at age 120; a west-of-UTC zone works; invalid zones give `null`. Both twins type-check apart from two errors that predate this change (the `insertReturning` cast against supabase-js's typed `select()`). **Deployed 2026-09-28.** `PORTAL_CLINIC_TIMEZONE` was set on the Pages production env with `wrangler pages secret put`, using the new Pages-scoped `CLOUDFLARE_PAGES_API_TOKEN` in the main `.env`; the existing 16 keys were untouched. It was also set as a Supabase secret and added to the main `.env`. Portal `3977cbe` was then pushed, and Pages deployment `4c923e9d` went live. An unauthenticated POST to it returns 401, not 500; the config checks run first, so this proves the zone is set and valid. **Verified live at 00:09 clinic time, while UTC was still on the 27th:** `test-cases-fn.mjs` was run against the production Pages Function itself (the new `CASES_FN_URL`) and passed 28/28. The set's `creation_date`, the note date and the DOB anchor all came out as **2026-09-28**, and the test rows are gone on both databases (reverse backlog 0). Found along the way, recorded in the portal CLAUDE.md in portal `f21477f`: the Edge twin `aligner-portal-cases` **was never deployed**, and its `PORTAL_CASE_*` secrets were never set on Supabase, so the twin was not deployed now either; production has only ever used the Pages Function. Also, the smoke test's self-minted service_role `apikey` is refused since the move to Supabase's new API keys, so the test now takes a real `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`.

## F4 · App shell + shared primitives · 2026-09-17

Scope read in full: the 17 shell/primitive components (`Modal` 297 · `ModalHeader` 96 · `ConfirmDialog` 53 · `UniversalHeader` 345 · `Navigation` 439 · `UserMenu` 147 · `ThemeToggle` 44 · `NavigationProgress` 26 · `PatientShell` 248 · `ContentRenderer` 377 · `PatientQuickSearch` 288 · `PatientSearchCombobox` 193 · `PhoneInput` 53 · `PhoneDisplay` 38 · `AlignerModeToggle` 47 · `SettingsTabNavigation` 82 · `PrintQueueIndicator` 202) — 2,975 LOC. Plus the cross-checks each one forced: the repo-wide `<Modal>` census (67 render sites across 59 files — drag handles, `ariaLabelledBy`, `closeOnEscape`, `closeOnBackdropClick`), every `useConfirm()` consumer (23 files) against the modals they open from, both patient-search consumers, `universal-header.css`'s four breakpoints, and `patientInfoRow`/`patientPhoneRow` in `shared/contracts/patient.contract.ts`.

**Runtime-verified on dev `:5273`** (Playwright, `scripts/e2e/auth.mjs`; no screenshots taken): the 412×915 header measurement below, the Escape-closes-the-stack repro on a real payment form, the dangling `aria-labelledby` on two live dialogs, and the sidebar flyout's focusability. The Escape repro wrote nothing — `invoices` for work 12979 still holds only the pre-existing row.

### Findings

| ID | Type | Sev | File | One-line | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FE-F4-1 | [BUG] | 🟠 | `Modal.tsx:141-153` | every open Modal owns a **document-level** Escape listener and `stopPropagation()` cannot stop a sibling listener on the same node — one Escape closes the whole stack, discarding the half-filled form underneath. Runtime-confirmed on the payment screen (2 dialogs → 0) | ✅ fixed (RA2) |
| FE-F4-2 | [BUG] | 🟡 | `Modal.tsx:97-98` | the `aria-labelledby` fallback is a fresh `useId()` pointing at an element that **never exists** — 8 files' dialogs, incl. `PaymentModal` and every `ConfirmDialog`, are nameless to a screen reader | ✅ fixed (RA3) |
| FE-F4-3 | [BUG] | 🟡 | `Navigation.tsx:70-80` | `patientsFolder` is cached in `localStorage` **forever** and the query is `enabled: !cachedFolder` — a Settings change to the UNC path never reaches a browser that already cached it | ✅ fixed (RA4) |
| FE-F4-4 | [BUG] | 🟡 | `PatientSearchCombobox.tsx:42` · `PatientQuickSearch.tsx:116` | the name jump-list matches **`startsWith`, case-sensitive**, and ignores the "Match from beginning of name only" checkbox rendered directly beneath it | ✅ fixed (RA4) |
| FE-F4-5 | [ENH] | 🟡 | `Navigation.tsx:296-311` | the More-actions flyout opens on **hover over a non-focusable `<div>`** — seven destinations are unreachable by keyboard; runtime-confirmed `tabIndex:-1`, no `role` | ✅ fixed (RA4) |
| FE-F4-6 | [ENH] | 🟡 | shared `Modal` (67 sites) | only 5 sites pass `closeOnBackdropClick` at all and there is no dirty-form guard anywhere — a stray backdrop click silently discards a filled payment / expense / lab-case form | ✅ fixed (RA5) |
| FE-F4-7 | [BUG] | 🔵 | `Navigation.tsx:56` · `PatientQuickSearch.tsx:82` | two `as` casts bridge contract-parsed reads into hand-written view-models (`PatientInfo`, `PatientOption`) — the shape CLAUDE.md bans; 4 of `PatientInfo`'s name fallbacks aren't on the contract at all | ✅ fixed (RA4) |
| FE-F4-8 | [BUG] | 🔵 | `PrintQueueIndicator.tsx:144` | `cn(styles.badgeChevron, { up: isExpanded })` emits the **literal** `up` while the rule lives in a CSS **Module** — the chevron never rotates and `.badgeChevron.up` is dead | ✅ fixed (RA4) |
| FE-F4-9 | [BUG] | 🔵 | `PatientShell.tsx:120` | reads `window.location.pathname` during render while `useLocation()` sits two lines above — under `v7_startTransition` the two can disagree mid-transition | ✅ fixed (RA4) |
| FE-F4-10 | [BUG] | 🔵 | `PatientShell.tsx:148-161` | a patient A→B switch fires `patient-cleared(A)` and `patient-loaded(B)` as two independent `sendBeacon`s; `chairEpoch` orders by **arrival**, so a transport reorder blanks the chair display for the 12 h TTL | ✅ fixed (RX1) |
| FE-F4-11 | [ENH] | 🔵 | `Modal.tsx:209-212` | the four handle-less modals turn their **whole body** into a drag surface and set `user-select:none` on `<body>` at pointerdown — text can't be selected in any of them | ✅ fixed (RA4) |
| FE-F4-12 | [ENH] | 🔵 | `Navigation.tsx:91-115,146-157` | two `try/catch`es wrap `window.location.href = 'csimaging:'/'dolphin:'`; an unregistered protocol doesn't throw, so the catch is unreachable and a missing handler gives the user **no feedback at all** | ✅ fixed (RA4) |
| FE-F4-13 | [ENH] | 🔵 | `PatientQuickSearch.tsx` | a 288-LOC three-`AsyncSelect` widget (plus `react-select/async`) survives for **one** consumer; `PatientSearchCombobox` replaced it everywhere else | ✅ fixed (RB4) |
| FE-F4-14 | [ENH] | 🔵 | `AlignerModeToggle.tsx:7` | takes a CSS-module object as a `styles: Record<string,string>` **prop**, with a `styles.active \|\| 'active'` global fallback — a prop-drilled stylesheet no dead-class scan can follow | ✅ fixed (RX1) |
| FE-F4-15 | [ENH] | 🔵 | `SettingsTabNavigation.tsx:63` · `AlignerModeToggle.tsx:15` | both are tab strips with no `role="tablist"` / `aria-selected` / `aria-current`, and `AlignerModeToggle`'s buttons have no `type="button"` | ✅ fixed (RA4) |
| FE-F4-16 | [ENH] | 🔵 | `UniversalHeader.tsx:201` | the Appointments tab's active rule is `p.includes('/appointment')`, so it lights up on `/patient/:id/appointments` **at the same time** as the patient tab | ✅ fixed (RA4) |
| FE-F4-17 | [ENH] | 🔵 | `ContentRenderer.tsx:369` vs `routes.config.tsx:411` | `Suspense` is deliberately scoped to the content body but the matching `RouteErrorBoundary` is not — one tab's failed chunk blanks the sidebar and breadcrumb with it. **F12 measured the concrete case** (a failed Photos chunk: 0 sidebar links, and the boundary calls the page "Patient Portal"): FE-F12-8(c) | ✅ fixed (RD) |
| FE-F4-18 | [DEAD] | 🔵 | `ContentRenderer.tsx:93` · `PatientShell.tsx:172-178` | `isNewPatient` is destructured to `_isNewPatient` and never read, and 4 of the 5 `params` fields PatientShell builds (`view`, `filter`, `workId`, `phone`) have no reader | ✅ fixed (RA4) |
| FE-F4-19 | [DEAD] | 🔵 | `utils/phoneFormatter.ts:39` · `PhoneDisplay.tsx:25` | `cleanPhone()` has **zero callers** while `PhoneDisplay` inlines the identical `replace(/[^\d]/g,'')` | ✅ fixed (RA4) |
| FE-F4-20 | [DEAD] | 🔵 | `PatientSearchCombobox.tsx:172,176` | listbox options carry `tabIndex={0}` and an Enter/Space `onKeyDown` that can never fire — `onBlur` closes the list before Tab can land on one | ✅ fixed (RA4) |
| FE-F4-21 | [BUG] | 🔵 | `LookupEditorModal.tsx:186` | **found in RX1:** the lookup editor's add/edit popover is a hand-rolled portal with its own **bubble-phase** document Escape listener, outside `Modal`'s Escape stack — opened inside `LookupManagerModal` (right-click a lab dropdown → Edit labs → Add New), ONE Escape closed the popover **and** the manager under it (runtime: 2 dialogs → 1). FE-F4-1's class, missed by its census because the popover is not a `<Modal>` | ✅ fixed (RX1) |

**FE-F4-1 — one Escape closes every modal on the stack.**
`Modal`'s Escape handler is registered on `document` by *each* open instance (`Modal.tsx:151`), and the handler calls `event.stopPropagation()` (`:146`). `stopPropagation` stops the event reaching *other nodes*; it does nothing to sibling listeners already registered on the **same** node, so when two Modals are open both handlers run and both call `onClose()`. Only `LookupManagerModal` opts out (`closeOnEscape={false}` plus its own capture-phase `stopImmediatePropagation`) — and it, along with `LookupContextMenu`, carries a comment explaining exactly this problem, which is the tell: the defect was found twice and patched locally twice instead of in the primitive.

Five live stacking sites remain, all of them a `<Modal>` that calls `useConfirm()` (which renders `ConfirmDialog` → another `<Modal>`): **`PaymentModal.tsx:749`** (underpayment warning), `LabCaseModal.tsx:258` (cancel case), `SetFormDrawer.tsx:262` and `BatchFormDrawer.tsx` (delete PDF), `CategoryManagerModal.tsx:71` (deactivate category). Drawers are not exempt — `isDrawer` disables *dragging*, not Escape.

Runtime repro (dev `:5273`, patient 7639 / work 12979, 1,500 USD balance): open **Add Payment**, register 100 with 50 received, Save → `document.querySelectorAll('[role="dialog"]')` is **2** ("Add Payment" + "Underpayment Warning"); press **Escape once** → **0**. The confirm correctly resolves `false` and nothing is written, but the whole payment form — amount, currency mode, received, change, date — is gone, and the front-desk user has to re-enter it. That is money-entry work destroyed by the keystroke a user reaches for to dismiss a warning. Fix belongs in `Modal.tsx`: a module-level stack of open instances, with only the top one responding to Escape, after which `LookupManagerModal`'s workaround can be deleted. `LookupContextMenu`'s **cannot** — it is a popover, not a Modal, so it stays out of the stack and still needs the capture phase to beat it. That is also why the stack must stay on the **bubble** phase: registering in capture would run before the context menu's handler and close the modal out from under it. **Fixed in RA2 — see the remediation log.**

**FE-F4-2 — every unnamed dialog claims a label that doesn't exist.**
`Modal` builds `labelledBy = ariaLabelledBy ?? fallbackTitleId` where `fallbackTitleId` is a bare `useId()` (`:97-98`) that is never rendered onto any element, then always emits `aria-labelledby={labelledBy}`. A dialog whose `aria-labelledby` points at a missing id has **no accessible name at all** — strictly worse than omitting the attribute, because the empty reference also suppresses the content fallback some screen readers would otherwise use. Confirmed live: on the payment screen both open dialogs report `aria-labelledby` (`_r_2_`, `_r_1_`) with `resolves: false`.

**Eight** files ship at least one `<Modal>` with no `ariaLabelledBy` — 10 render sites: `AdminUserManagement`, `ConfirmDialog` (so **every** `useConfirm()` in the app), `LabelPreviewModal` (×2), `PaymentModal`, `TransferWorkModal`, `photo-editor/PhotoEditor` (×2), `routes/StandPOS`, `routes/StandSalesHistory`. (Corrected in RA3 from "twelve": the census had counted `LookupContextMenu`, `LookupManagerModal`, `files/FilePreviewModal` and `pages/aligner/Announcements`, but the first renders no `<Modal>` at all and the other three already pass a resolving `ariaLabelledBy` — the four matches were `<Modal>` mentioned in their docblocks.) `PaymentModal` is the sharpest case: it renders a `<ModalHeader>` with a perfectly good visible title and simply never passes `titleId`/`ariaLabelledBy`. Fix: make the fallback `undefined` rather than a dangling id, and name each of the 10 off the heading it already renders. **Fixed in RA3 — see the remediation log.**

**FE-F4-3 — the patients-folder UNC is cached per device, forever.**
`Navigation` reads `localStorage.getItem('patientsFolder')` during render, runs `patientsFolderQuery()` only `enabled: !cachedFolder`, and writes the fetched value back (`:70-80`). Nothing in the tree ever removes that key — a repo-wide grep for `patientsFolder` finds exactly one `getItem` and one `setItem`, both here, and `patientsFolderQuery` has no other consumer. Failure scenario: the clinic moves its share and an admin updates `PatientsFolder` in Settings; every browser that has opened a patient page since install keeps building `explorer:\\OLD\Clinic1\<id>` and the folder button opens nothing, with no way to recover short of clearing site data. The value is ordinary server state with a long `staleTime` — the localStorage layer buys nothing React Query doesn't already give. Related and unenforced: the whole scheme depends on the stored value ending in a separator (`${patientsFolder}${personId}`, `:219`); today's row is `\\CLINIC\Clinic1\`, but nothing validates it, so a Settings edit that drops the trailing `\` silently yields `\\CLINIC\Clinic17639`.

**FE-F4-4 — the jump-list and the checkbox under it disagree.**
Both patient searches match names with `p.name?.startsWith(input)` (`PatientSearchCombobox.tsx:42`, `PatientQuickSearch.tsx:116`) while matching phone and ID with `.includes()`. Two consequences. (a) `startsWith` is case-**sensitive**, and the server side is `citext`: typing `ali` offers nothing while pressing Enter finds `Ali`. (b) `PatientManagement` renders a **"Match from beginning of name only"** checkbox (`:594`) that switches the *table* search between prefix and contains — the combobox above it is hard-wired to prefix and never reads that flag, so with the box unchecked the dropdown stays empty while the results table below fills with matches. Failure scenario: front desk types a patient's second word ("كاروان" for "سيما كاروان"), sees an empty jump list, and concludes the patient isn't in the system. Fix: pass the flag down and use `includes` when it is off; fold the case-fold in at the same time.

**FE-F4-5 — seven patient destinations are mouse-only.**
The More-actions control is a `<div className="more-actions-btn">` inside a `<div className="more-actions-wrapper">` whose only triggers are `onMouseEnter`/`onMouseLeave` (`:296-311`). Neither div has `tabIndex`, `role` or a key handler — runtime-confirmed on `/patient/7639/works`: `more-actions-btn` reports `tagName:"DIV"`, `tabIndex:-1`, `role:null`, and the only focusable items in `.sidebar-footer` are the CS Imaging and Folder rows (both of which *do* carry `role="button" tabIndex={0}` plus Enter/Space). So Compare, Presentation, X-rays, Scans, Send to 3Shape, Open in Dolphin and New Photo Session cannot be reached with a keyboard, and the sidebar is their only entry point. The fix is the pattern already used twice in the same file, plus opening the flyout on focus/click rather than hover only.

**FE-F4-6 — the shared Modal has no unsaved-work guard, and its default is dismiss.**
Of the 67 `<Modal>` render sites, **five** pass `closeOnBackdropClick` at all: `ConfirmDialog` (hard `false`) and four that gate it on an in-flight flag (`NewWorkComponent` ×2 `!loading`, `AdminUserManagement` `!resetting`, `StandSalesHistory` `!voiding`). `closeOnEscape` is opted out of exactly once (`LookupManagerModal`). *(Both counts moved after F4 read them: the census is **73** render sites by RA5, and `LookupManagerModal`'s `closeOnEscape={false}` was deleted in RA2 when the escape stack made it unnecessary — so by RA5 the only dismissal opt-outs left were the four in-flight ones.)* Everything else — `PaymentModal`, `ExpenseModal`, `LabCaseModal`, `TaskFormModal`, `ItemFormModal`, `RestockModal`, the three aligner drawers, `EditTimepointModal`, … — dismisses on a stray click outside or an Escape with no confirmation, and none of them tracks whether the form is dirty. This is the primitive's documented default rather than a local mistake, which is why it is one finding and not thirty: the shared `Modal` should offer a `confirmOnDismiss`/`isDirty` opt-in (routed through the existing `useConfirm`) so a data-entry modal can refuse to vanish. Pairs with FE-F4-1 — same keystroke, same lost form. **Fixed in RA5 — see the remediation log.**

**FE-F4-7 — two casts past the fail-loud guard.**
`Navigation.tsx:56` does `const patientInfo = (piData ?? null) as PatientInfo | null` over a `patientInfoQuery()` result, where `PatientInfo` is a local interface declaring eight optional name fields plus `[key: string]: unknown`. `patientInfoRow` (`shared/contracts/patient.contract.ts:73`) actually guarantees `patient_name`, `first_name`, `last_name` and `name` — and has no `patientName`, `Name`, `FullName` or `fullName` at all, so four of the six fallbacks in `handleOpenCSImaging` (`:94-100`) are unreachable. `PatientQuickSearch.tsx:82` does the same with `(data as PatientOption[] | undefined)`, where `PatientOption` weakens the contract's `name: z.string()` to `name?: string`, which is what forces the `p.name || ''` at `:103` and `:118`. Both are the pattern CLAUDE.md names explicitly ("Never bridge a parsed read into a view-model with an `as` cast"); the fix in both cases is to delete the interface and use `z.infer`.

**FE-F4-8 — the queue chevron never turns.**
`PrintQueueIndicator.tsx:144` writes `cn(styles.badgeChevron, { up: isExpanded })`. `cn`'s object form emits the **key** as a literal class, so the element gets `_badgeChevron_xxx up`, while `PrintQueueIndicator.module.css:60` declares `.badgeChevron.up { transform: rotate(180deg) }` — compiled by CSS Modules to `._badgeChevron_xxx._up_xxx`. The literal `up` can never match it. Expanding the print queue leaves the chevron pointing down; the rule at `:60` is dead. One-character class of bug worth grepping for elsewhere (`cn(..., { <bare-key>: … })` against a module).

**FE-F4-9 — `window.location` read during render, two lines under `useLocation()`.**
`PatientShell.tsx:120` computes `isDiagnosisRoute` from `window.location.pathname.endsWith('/diagnosis')` while `location` from `useLocation()` is already in scope (`:72`). The router updates `history` before it commits the new location into context, so during a `v7_startTransition` render the component can see the *new* `window.location` alongside the *old* `page` param — `effectivePage` flips to `'diagnosis'` while `workId` is still the previous route's. Nothing user-visible has been reproduced, but the correct value is sitting right there and the render is otherwise pure.

**FE-F4-10 — the chair display can be blanked by beacon reordering.**
Switching patients on a chair-configured PC keeps `PatientShell` mounted, so React runs the effect's cleanup (`notifyChairDisplay('/api/chair-display/patient-cleared', { chairId })`) and then the new effect (`…/patient-loaded`, `{ chairId, personId: B }`) — two independent `navigator.sendBeacon` POSTs. `sendBeacon` guarantees delivery, not ordering, and the server assigns `chairEpoch` **on arrival** (`services/messaging/sse-broadcaster.ts:63,77`), so a reorder makes CLEAR the winner: `chairCurrentPatient.delete(chairId)` runs last and the kiosk goes blank with patient B open at the chair, staying blank until the staff navigate away and back (the cache has a 12 h TTL and no re-request path). The epoch already exists — it just needs to be client-supplied and monotonic per chair, so the server can drop a stale CLEAR the same way it drops a stale LOAD.

**FE-F4-11 — handle-less modals eat text selection.**
`Modal`'s drag start (`:209-212`) only restricts itself to `[data-modal-drag-handle]` *if the modal contains one*; otherwise any pointerdown on a non-interactive area begins a drag and sets `document.body.style.userSelect = 'none'` (`:226`). Four consumers render no `<ModalHeader>` and therefore no handle — `ConfirmDialog`, `files/FilePreviewModal`, `LabelPreviewModal`, `photo-editor/PhotoEditor`'s two confirms — and all four are exactly the shapes whose *content* is text the user might want to copy (a confirm's explanation, a previewed text file, the label list). Giving those four bespoke headers `data-modal-drag-handle` fixes it without touching the primitive; the memo at `[[modalheader-draggable-modals]]` already records them as deliberate non-migrations, so the attribute is the minimal change.

**FE-F4-12 — a catch that cannot fire, over a failure that has no other signal.**
`handleOpenCSImaging` and `handleOpenDolphin` (`:91-115`, `:146-157`) wrap `window.location.href = '<scheme>:…'` in `try/catch` and `toast.error(...)` on failure. Assigning an unregistered scheme to `location.href` does not throw — the browser either shows its own "no app" dialog or does nothing — so the catch is unreachable and the toast never appears. The user-visible consequence is the inverse of the code's intent: on a PC where the protocol handler was never installed (the case `ProtocolHandlersSettings` exists for), the button is completely silent. Either drop the dead catch or replace it with a real signal — e.g. a short `blur`/`visibilitychange` race that concludes "handler not installed" when the page never loses focus.

**FE-F4-13 — 288 lines and a dependency for one caller.** `PatientQuickSearch` (three `AsyncSelect`s from `react-select/async`, each with its own `loadOptions`, its own RTL `styles` object and its own `noOptionsMessage`) is imported as a component by exactly one file: `TransferWorkModal`. `PatientManagement` imports only its `PatientOption` *type*, and `PatientSearchCombobox` — the 193-line replacement that does the same job in one field — imports the same type. That answers F4's "why are there two": one is the successor, and the predecessor was left behind on a single screen. Folding `TransferWorkModal` onto the combobox retires the component, the `react-select/async` entry point, and (with FE-F4-4) one of the two copies of the matching rules; `PatientOption` then belongs in `types/`, not in a component module.

## F3 · Contexts, shared hooks, error boundaries · 2026-09-16

Scope read in full: `contexts/` (7), `hooks/` (17), `components/error-boundaries/` (4 `.tsx` + their 2 CSS modules) — 4,681 LOC. Plus the cross-checks each surface forced: every `useGlobalState`/`usePrintQueue` consumer, the server side of the QR-viewer gate (`services/messaging/whatsapp.ts`, `sse-whatsapp.ts`, `routes/api/whatsapp-session.routes.ts`), the repo-wide browser-locale date census, and the weekend definition on both sides of the wire.

### Findings

| ID | Type | Sev | File | One-line | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FE-F3-1 | [BUG] | 🟡 | `MonthlyCalendarGrid.tsx:62` | the monthly calendar shades **Saturday + Sunday** as the weekend in a clinic whose week is Sat–Thu with **Friday** off — two working days tinted, and the closed day isn't even in the grid | ✅ fixed (RA1) |
| FE-F3-2 | [BUG] | 🟡 | `hooks/useExpenses.ts:223,243` | an expense edit/delete that routes to the **approval queue** invalidates nothing — neither the submitter's `MyApprovalsBadge` nor the admin's `ApprovalsBell` moves for up to 5 minutes | ✅ fixed (RA1) |
| FE-F3-3 | [BUG] | 🟡 | 36 files / 53 sites | date formatting that takes the **browser's** locale bypasses the `ar-IQ-u-nu-latn` digit pin — Arabic-Indic digits on translated screens on an Arabic-locale Windows box | ✅ fixed (RX1) |
| FE-F3-4 | [BUG] | 🟡 | `contexts/ToastContext.tsx:102` | the toast container has **no `aria-live`/`role="status"`** — the channel that replaced `alert()` app-wide is silent to a screen reader | ✅ fixed (RA1) |
| FE-F3-5 | [BUG] | 🟡 | `hooks/useWhatsAppAuth.ts:166,351` | **two** render-phase state syncs over the same two inputs, with different rules — both load-bearing, neither complete | ✅ fixed (RX1) |
| FE-F3-6 | [ENH] | 🟡 | `contexts/GlobalStateContext.tsx:236` | one context carries three unrelated concerns, its `value` is the only unmemoized one in the tree, and **8 of its 12 members have no consumer** | ✅ fixed (RX1) |
| FE-F3-7 | [BUG] | 🔵 | `components/error-boundaries/GlobalErrorBoundary.tsx:28` | "Reset and Reload" clears three `localStorage` keys that **exist nowhere in the repo** — it is a plain reload wearing a reset's label | ✅ fixed (RA1) |
| FE-F3-8 | [BUG] | 🔵 | `hooks/useAppointments.ts:235,236,246` | three `as` casts bridge a parsed contract payload into a hand-written `Appointment`/`AppointmentStats` view-model — the exact shape CLAUDE.md bans | ✅ fixed (RC1) |
| FE-F3-9 | [BUG] | 🔵 | `hooks/useAppointmentsSync.ts:59` | `isViewingToday` is derived at **render** time, and nothing re-renders at 00:00 — an overnight tab keeps claiming "Live" over yesterday's list | ✅ fixed (RC2, via FE-F11-8a; ledger row closed in RB4) |
| FE-F3-10 | [ENH] | 🔵 | `contexts/ConfirmContext.tsx:29` | `confirm()` resolves the previous promise **inside a `setState` updater** — an impure updater that React double-invokes in StrictMode | ✅ fixed (RA1) |
| FE-F3-11 | [ENH] | 🔵 | `hooks/useWhatsAppSync.ts:112` | `requestInitialState` dedupes by date against an endpoint that **takes no date** | ✅ fixed (RX1) |
| FE-F3-12 | [ENH] | 🔵 | `hooks/useStand.ts`, `useExpenses.ts` | one shared `loading` flag across 5 mutations, fire-and-forget `void invalidateQueries`, and `useStandCategoryMutations` with no error surface at all | ✅ fixed (RX1) |
| FE-F3-13 | [ENH] | 🔵 | `hooks/useLookupManager.tsx:167` | "Edit values" / "Manage …" are English literals rendered on **translated** screens, and the eslint i18n ratchet structurally cannot see them | ✅ fixed (RX1) |
| FE-F3-14 | [ENH] | 🔵 | `contexts/PrintQueueContext.tsx` + `LabelPreviewModal.tsx:231,396` | the modal re-implements `getGroupedQueue` and `buildLabelsForPrint`; `RootLayout` re-implements `isModalOpen` — the context's versions are the dead ones | ✅ fixed (RX1) |
| FE-F3-15 | [DEAD] | 🔵 | `contexts/GlobalStateContext.tsx` | ~80 LOC of unreachable state: `currentPatient` + 2 updaters, `appointmentsCache` + its LRU bound, `setUser`, both WhatsApp setters, and 3 exported types | ✅ fixed (RA1) |
| FE-F3-16 | [DEAD] | 🔵 | `components/error-boundaries/ErrorBoundary.tsx:108-140` | the class's **default fallback is unreachable** — both wrappers pass `fallback`; with it die `showDetails`, `onReset`, the `navigate` prop (and the `useNavigate` that feeds it) and 109 lines of `ErrorBoundary.module.css` | ✅ fixed (RA1) |
| FE-F3-17 | [DEAD] | 🔵 | `hooks/useWhatsAppAuth.ts:85,150` | `connectionAttempts` is **only ever set to 0**, and `fetchQRCode` has zero consumers — both are returned to the UI | ✅ fixed (RA1) |
| FE-F3-18 | [DEAD] | 🔵 | `query/useApiMutation.ts:66` | `isInvalidStateTransition` — CLAUDE.md's documented shared predicate, with its own unit tests — has **zero callers**; its one consumer hand-rolls it byte-for-byte | ✅ fixed (RA1) |
| FE-F3-19 | [DEAD] | 🔵 | `contexts/PrintQueueContext.tsx` | `toggleLogo`, `updateLabels`, `buildLabelsForPrint`, `isModalOpen`/`setIsModalOpen`, the `addedAt` field and the `RichLabel` type: no consumer | ✅ fixed (RA1) |
| FE-F3-20 | [DEAD] | 🔵 | `hooks/useDateManager.ts:179,100` | a comment that attributes the send window to a "database stored procedure" (there are none — CLAUDE.md), and `formatDateLabel`'s unused `_currentDate` | ✅ fixed (RA1) |

**FE-F3-1 — the calendar tints two working days as the weekend.**
`MonthlyCalendarGrid` computes `isWeekend(date)` as `day === 0 || day === 6` — "Sunday = 0, Saturday = 6", the Western weekend — and applies `styles.weekend` (a grey gradient) to those cells. Eleven lines above it, the same component declares `const dayHeaders = ['Sat','Sun','Mon','Tue','Wed','Thu']` with the comment *"starting with Saturday, excluding Friday"*. Both halves of the contradiction are in one file: the grid is a **six-column, Friday-less week**, so Saturday and Sunday are ordinary working days and the one closed day never renders a cell at all. Every other weekend decision in the tree agrees with the header and not with `isWeekend`: `services/business/CalendarViewService.ts:438` skips `getDay() === 5` ("Friday … is a non-working day with no column in the 6-day [week]"), `public/js/utils/calendarDate.ts:53` steps over Friday, and `useDateManager.getDefaultDate()` pushes Thursday's reminder target to Saturday *because Saturday is a work day*. Failure scenario: open `/calendar` in month view — the two busiest columns of an Iraqi clinic week are greyed as though closed, and a front-desk user reading the grid for a free day is being told the wrong thing by the styling. (Note for whoever fixes it: **CLAUDE.md's own prose says "Fri+Sat weekends"** in the ~2M-row ceiling arithmetic, which is the opposite of what the code does. One of the two is wrong and the product owner has to say which — if Saturday really is closed, the bug is in `CalendarViewService`/`calendarDate`/`useDateManager`, not here.) Owner: **F10**.

**FE-F3-2 — the approval you just submitted is invisible for five minutes.**
`updateExpense` (`:223`) and `deleteExpense` (`:243`) read the server's maker-checker outcome and short-circuit on `pending`: `if (data.outcome === 'pending') return { outcome: 'pending' };` — deliberately *before* `invalidateQueries({ queryKey: qk.expenses.all() })`, which is correct, because a held edit changes no expense row. What it also skips is any signal that an **approval request was created**. The approvals surfaces do not use React Query at all: `MyApprovalsBadge` and `ApprovalsBell` poll on their own `REFRESH_MS = 5 * 60 * 1000` timer and listen for the `approvals:changed` window event (FE-F1-10's CustomEvent bus). Repo-wide, `notifyApprovalsChanged()` is called from **exactly one place** — `ApprovalsBell.tsx:117`, i.e. when an admin *resolves* a request. Nothing that *creates* one ever fires it. Failure scenario: a front-desk user edits a three-week-old expense, the server holds it, the UI says "sent for approval" — and their own "My approvals" badge still reads the old count for up to five minutes, as does the admin's bell, so the request looks lost. The same hole applies to every other hold-tier write (F8 owns the expense screens, F5 owns the bells); the fix is one `notifyApprovalsChanged()` on the `pending` branch, or — better, and what FE-F1-10 argues for — moving the bells onto `qk.approvals.*` so an ordinary invalidation reaches them.

**FE-F3-3 — 53 date formats that take the browser's locale instead of the app's.**
`utils/formatters.ts` is the SSoT and gets this exactly right: every call passes an explicit `LANGUAGES[lang].locale`, and the Arabic entry is **`'ar-IQ-u-nu-latn'`** — Iraq Arabic with the Latin-digit numbering system pinned by BCP-47 extension, annotated "Western 0-9 — user decision". Against that, `grep` finds **53 calls in 36 files** of the form `toLocaleDateString()`, `toLocaleDateString(undefined, …)`, `toLocaleTimeString()` or `toLocaleString(undefined, …)`, which resolve to the *browser's* default locale and therefore to the **host OS's** locale. On an `ar-IQ` Windows install — an entirely ordinary configuration for this product's market — those render `٢٠٢٦/٠٩/١٦` while the money beside them renders `1,250,000`. `ViewPatientInfo.tsx` shows the split inside one file: `toLocaleDateString(undefined, …)` at `:145` and `:158`, `toLocaleString('en-US')` at `:195`, `:203`, `:284` — the author pinned the digits for money and not for dates. The translated screens carrying it are `ExpenseTable.tsx:32`, `WorkComponent.tsx:424`, `WorkDetailItem`, `ViewPatientInfo` and `DailyInvoicesModal`; the rest are untranslated screens where the same OS-locale leak still applies. This is upkeep rather than a live bug *on the machines in this clinic today* (they run en-US), which is why it is 🟡 and not 🟠 — but it is exactly the class of assumption CLAUDE.md's "don't tune to this clinic" rule exists for. Full census in the clean list below; each owning session fixes its own files by routing through `utils/formatters.ts`.

**FE-F3-4 — the app's entire async-status channel is inaudible.**
`ToastContainer` renders `<div className="toast-container">` with no `role`, no `aria-live` and no `aria-atomic`, and `public/css/components/toast.css` adds none (checked: the file has no `[role]`/`[aria-` selector, and no element anywhere in `public/js` carries `aria-live`). CLAUDE.md's rule is "Toast, not `alert()`" — and `alert()` is, whatever else is wrong with it, announced and focus-stealing by construction. Every success confirmation, every save failure, every "Patient state changed — refreshing" in the app now lands in a `<div>` that assistive tech has no reason to read. The fix is two attributes on the container (`role="status" aria-live="polite"`, and `aria-live="assertive"` for the error variant if it is worth splitting the container). Related and free while it is open: the close button's `aria-label="Close"` is a hard-coded English literal that renders on the Arabic screens, and the container has no cap — a `catch` in a loop can stack toasts without bound.

**FE-F3-5 — two render-phase syncs of the same two values, with different rules.**
`useWhatsAppAuth` keys `authState` off `qrCode` + `clientReady` from `GlobalStateContext` in *two* separate render-phase blocks. Block 1 (`prevQrReady1`, `:166`) fires when either input changes: `clientReady → AUTHENTICATED`, else `qrCode → QR_REQUIRED` unless already AUTHENTICATED. Block 2 (`prevQrReady2`, `:351`) fires when either input **or `authState` itself** changes, and applies a *narrower* rule: it only moves to `QR_REQUIRED` from `AUTHENTICATED` or from `CHECKING_SESSION`. So block 2 re-runs immediately after every `setAuthState` block 1 performs, and each covers a case the other misses — block 1 catches "QR arrived while RESTORING", block 2 catches "authState moved to CHECKING_SESSION while a QR was already in hand". Both are therefore live, and the file has four *more* writers of the same variable (`handleInitialState`, the six SSE handlers, the three action handlers, and a bare 3-second `setTimeout` at `:128` that is never cleared). Failure mode is not a today-bug — it is that the next state added to `AUTH_STATES` will be handled by one block and not the other, silently. This is one `useReducer` over `{qrCode, clientReady, transport, serverState}`, and it is the single highest-value altitude fix in this session's scope.

**FE-F3-6 — one context, three concerns, eight dead members.**
`GlobalStateContext` publishes 12 members. A repo-wide grep of every `useGlobalState()` call site (9 files) shows exactly **three** are ever destructured: `user` (7 screens), `whatsappClientReady` (3), `whatsappQrCode` (1). The other eight — `setUser`, `currentPatient`, `updateCurrentPatient`, `clearCurrentPatient`, `setWhatsappClientReady`, `setWhatsappQrCode`, `appointmentsCache`, `updateAppointmentsCache` — have **no consumer anywhere** (filed separately as FE-F3-15; the `currentPatient` hits in `UniversalHeader` are a local of the same name). Two consequences beyond the dead weight. (a) It is the only context in the tree whose `value` is a bare object literal — `ToastContext` (`:188`), `ThemeContext`, `LanguageContext` and `FontContext` all `useMemo` it, and `ToastContext` carries a comment explaining exactly why ("makes any consumer effect with `toast` in its deps re-fire — a refetch storm"). (b) Because all three concerns share one value, a WhatsApp QR rotation re-renders every screen that only wanted `user`: while the client is unpaired, whatsapp-web.js mints a new QR roughly every 20 s, the server pushes `whatsapp_qr_updated`, and `setWhatsappQrCode(newString)` invalidates the context for `UniversalHeader`, `Dashboard`, `StatisticsComponent`, `WorkComponent`, `NewWorkComponent`, `Expenses` and `PatientSets`. Blast radius is genuinely narrow — the server blanks `qr` by role, so for a non-finance user the value stays `null`, React's `Object.is` bail-out fires and nothing re-renders — so this costs only admin/finance sessions, only while WhatsApp is unpaired. Split it into `useAuthUser` (or just `authMeQuery` directly — it is already the source) and a `WhatsAppStatusContext`, and memoize both.

**FE-F3-7 — a reset button that resets nothing.**
`GlobalErrorFallback`'s secondary action reads `const appKeys = ['shwan_user_prefs','shwan_nav_state','shwan_last_route']; appKeys.forEach(k => localStorage.removeItem(k)); window.location.reload();`. Those three strings appear **nowhere else in the repo** — not in `public/js`, not in the entry HTMLs, not on the server. The keys the app actually writes are `shwan_theme`, `shwan_language`, `shwan_arabic_font` and `chairId` (plus `sessionStorage`'s `currentUser` and `labelPrintQueue`). So "Reset and Reload" is byte-for-byte equivalent to "Reload Application" beside it, offered to a user whose app has just crashed hard enough to escape every route boundary. Either delete the button or make it clear what it should actually clear — noting that the real preference keys are per-device display settings that are *not* plausible crash causes, while `sessionStorage['labelPrintQueue']` (arbitrary persisted JSON replayed into a component on every load) is. Two more in the same file while it is open: the fallback carries **130 lines of inline `<style>`** with literal hex colours (`#667eea`, `background: white`) in a repo whose rule is CSS Modules + design tokens, so the crash screen is stuck light-themed; and `GlobalErrorBoundary` passes `showDetails={false}` alongside a `fallback`, where `showDetails` is only ever read by the default fallback the `fallback` prop replaces (FE-F3-16).

**FE-F3-8 — the funnel parses it, then the hook asserts over the result.**
`useAppointments`'s read is exemplary right up to the return: `fetchDailyAppointments` passes `{ schema: dailyAppointments.response }`, so the payload is contract-parsed at the boundary. Then `:235`, `:236` and `:246` hand it out as `(data?.allAppointments ?? []) as Appointment[]`, `… as Appointment[]` and `… as AppointmentStats`, where `Appointment`/`AppointmentStats` are hand-written interfaces declared in the same file. CLAUDE.md is explicit — *"Never bridge a parsed read into a view-model with an `as`/`as unknown` cast … nor re-wrap it in a hand-written response `interface`"* — and the reason applies here: the interfaces assert `patient_name`, `phone`, `patient_type_id` and a `total/checkedIn/absent/waiting` stat block that the compiler is no longer checking against `appointment.contract.ts`. Drop a field from the contract and the screen keeps compiling and renders `undefined`. (F0's census counted `as unknown as` — 11, none of them this shape — so plain `as X[]` bridges like these three are the gap in that baseline. The same pattern is worth grepping for in F5–F22: `as \w+\[\]` immediately after a schema-carrying read.)

**FE-F3-9 — the overnight tab.**
`useAppointmentsSync` gates every real-time behaviour on `const isViewingToday = currentDate === getTodayDate()`, evaluated in the render body. Nothing schedules a render at midnight — there is no rollover timer in the hook, and the only recurring work is the 5-minute `triggerRecoveryFetch` interval, which refetches but does not re-derive the date. So a front-desk PC left on `/appointments` overnight (the normal state of a reception machine) wakes up with: `isViewingToday` still `true` from yesterday's last render, the SSE stream still open, the indicator still reading **Live**, and effect 4's `receivedDate === expectedDate` guard now discarding every broadcast for the *new* day. The list on screen is yesterday's, and it looks authoritative. It self-heals the moment anything re-renders the component with the date recomputed, which is why this is 🔵 rather than 🟡 — but "anything" is not guaranteed to happen before the first patient arrives. A midnight `setTimeout` that bumps the date (or a `Date`-keyed interval) closes it. **F11** owns whether `DailyAppointments` should also roll its selected date forward.

**FE-F3-10 — a side effect inside a state updater.** `ConfirmProvider.confirm()` replaces a pending dialog with `setPending((prev) => { prev?.resolve(false); return { message, options, resolve }; })`. Updater functions must be pure: React may call them more than once for a single update, and under StrictMode in dev it deliberately does. `resolve` is idempotent so nothing breaks today, but the pattern is one refactor away from being wrong (any non-idempotent cleanup in that position fires twice), and the same effect is available safely by reading `pending` from a ref or resolving before the `setPending` call.

**FE-F3-11 — a dedupe key the request does not contain.** `requestInitialState(dateToRequest = currentDateRef.current)` guards on `lastRequestedDateRef.current === dateToRequest` and then fetches **`GET /api/wa/initial-state`** — a URL with no date in it, served by a handler (`routes/api/whatsapp-session.routes.ts`) that reads only `req.session` and `messageState`. So the "per-date dedupe" is really a fetch-once-per-date-change throttle over a date-independent resource. The visible consequence is the inverse of what the comment claims: once a send finishes and the user stays on the same date, `requestInitialState()` is a permanent no-op (the ref still holds that date), so `sendingProgress` can only ever be re-synced from REST by changing the date or bouncing the transport. Live SSE events cover it in practice, which is why it is 🔵.

**FE-F3-12 — three shapes of mutation ergonomics in two hooks.** (a) `useStandItemMutations` exposes a single `loading` for five operations and `useExpenseMutations` one for three; two concurrent calls mean the first to settle flips `loading` false while the other is still in flight, so a spinner or a disabled Save can come back early. (b) Every invalidation is `void queryClient.invalidateQueries(...)` — fire-and-forget, so `await createSale(...)` resolves before the refetch starts, and a screen that closes a modal on that `await` renders the stale list for a beat. `query/useApiMutation.ts` awaits its invalidations before the caller's `onSuccess` precisely to avoid this, which is the argument for FE-F2-13's adoption point. (c) `useStandCategoryMutations` is `try/finally` with **no `catch`, no `setError`, and no `error` in its return type** — the rejection does propagate to the caller, but the hook is the odd one of the three and a caller that forgets a `catch` gets an unhandled rejection instead of the `httpErrorMessage` string its siblings provide.

**FE-F3-13 — an i18n hole the ratchet cannot see.** `useLookupManager` defaults `menuLabel = 'Edit values'` and titles its modal `Manage <displayName>`. It is wired into the expense forms — a module the tracker lists as fully translated — so right-clicking the Lab or Category dropdown on the Arabic `/expenses` screen opens an English context menu inside an RTL page. The eslint `i18next/no-literal-string` ratchet enforces translation per *file*, and `hooks/useLookupManager.tsx` is not (and, as a shared hook serving both translated and untranslated call sites, cannot simply be) on the list — so the rule that is supposed to prevent exactly this is structurally blind to it. The fix belongs to whoever translates the lookup editor (F21): take the labels as props from the call site, or move them into the `common` namespace and let the hook call `useTranslation`.

**FE-F3-14 — the context's helpers lost to copies.** `PrintQueueContext` exports `getGroupedQueue()` (group queue items by `personId`) and `buildLabelsForPrint()` (flatten items into `RichLabel[]`), and declares `isModalOpen`/`setIsModalOpen` for the print modal. `LabelPreviewModal.tsx` declares its **own** `PatientGroup` interface (`:62`), its **own** `RichLabel` interface (`:74`), its own grouping `useMemo` (`:231`) and its own `buildRichLabels` (`:396`); `RootLayout.tsx:56` holds its own `showQueueModal` useState and casts the context with `usePrintQueue() as { queue: QueuedItem[]; clearQueue: () => void }`. Neither copy is wrong today — they are the same logic — which is precisely the problem: a label-format change has to be made in two places, and the context's copies are the ones with no test, no caller and no reason for a maintainer to look at them. Consolidate on the context (F20 owns label printing).

**FE-F3-15 — the dead two-thirds of `GlobalStateContext`.** With the three live members established in FE-F3-6, what is left is unreachable: `currentPatient` + `updateCurrentPatient` + `clearCurrentPatient` (a `useState` and two setters, no reader); `appointmentsCache` + `updateAppointmentsCache` **plus `MAX_CACHED_APPOINTMENT_DATES` and the whole LRU-eviction body written to bound it** — a cache with no writer and no reader, superseded by `qk.appointments.daily`; `setUser` (whose comment still says "consumers still call `setUser`, e.g. after login" — none do); and `setWhatsappClientReady`/`setWhatsappQrCode`. The exported types `PatientData`, `AppointmentData` and `AppointmentsCache` are referenced only inside this file. ~80 LOC, and deleting it is what makes FE-F3-6's split a small change instead of a large one.

**FE-F3-16 — a fallback UI that nothing can reach.** `ErrorBoundary.render()` returns `this.props.fallback` when one is given and its own 35-line default otherwise. Both users pass one: `GlobalErrorBoundary` (`<ErrorBoundary fallback={<GlobalErrorFallback />} showDetails={false}>`) and `RouteErrorBoundary` (`fallback={<RouteErrorFallback routeName={…} />}`), and the repo has no third. So the default markup, its three buttons (two of which — "Return to Dashboard" and "Go to Dashboard" — are behaviourally identical once `resetKey` auto-clears on navigation), `handleReset`, the `onReset` prop, the `showDetails` prop, the `navigate` prop **and the `useNavigate()` in `ErrorBoundaryWithNavigate` that exists only to fill it** are all dead, together with the 109-line `ErrorBoundary.module.css` and its 10 generated class names. What the wrapper actually contributes is `resetKey={location.pathname}`, which is load-bearing and must survive the cleanup. (The file's `componentDidCatch` — chunk self-heal, then `reportClientError` — is correct and is separately recorded as clean.)

**FE-F3-17 — a counter that is only ever zero.** `useWhatsAppAuth` declares `const [connectionAttempts, setConnectionAttempts] = useState(0)` and returns it in the hook's public shape. `setConnectionAttempts` is called three times — `:184`, `:206`, `:280` — and every one of them passes `0`. There is no increment anywhere. A repo-wide grep finds **no consumer** of `connectionAttempts` either, so it is dead twice over; the same grep clears `actions.fetchQRCode` (`:150`, 11 lines including a deliberate 404-is-normal branch), which no caller invokes.

**FE-F3-18 — the documented shared predicate has no callers.** `query/useApiMutation.ts:66` exports `isInvalidStateTransition(err)`; CLAUDE.md names it in the React Query section as "the shared 400-conflict predicate for silent-reload recovery"; `useApiMutation.test.ts` covers it across status/shape permutations. Repo-wide, the only references are its declaration, its doc comment and its tests. Meanwhile `useAppointments.recoverFromConflict` (`:144-157`) — the one place in the app that needs it, and the case the doc comment describes ("a missed appointments SSE") — re-implements it inline: same `status !== 400` guard, same `data.details.code === 'INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION'` read. Import it and delete the copy; the tests then cover live code.

**FE-F3-19 — `PrintQueueContext`'s unused surface.** Beyond FE-F3-14's duplicated pair: `toggleLogo(id)` and `updateLabels(id, labels)` have no call site (the only `toggleLogo` hits in the tree belong to the compare engine), `isModalOpen`/`setIsModalOpen` are unused (RootLayout holds its own), the `addedAt: Date.now()` written into every queue item is never read, and `RichLabel` is shadowed by the modal's own copy. Also here, uncontroversially: `generateId()` uses the deprecated `String.prototype.substr`.

**FE-F3-20 — two stale notes in `useDateManager`.** `getDateSendability`'s comment says "The database stored procedure only returns messages for tomorrow or day after tomorrow" — CLAUDE.md: "No stored procedures/triggers/functions for app logic (all in TypeScript)", and the rule the code enforces is a plain `daysFromToday === 1 || === 2`. And `formatDateLabel(date, _currentDate)` takes a second parameter it does not use; the caller passes `currentDate` to it at `:171` and separately uses the same value for `isDefault` at `:173`.

---

## F2 · Server-state layer (query/router) · 2026-09-16

Scope read in full: `query/` (7 files incl. 3 tests), `router/` (2), `App.tsx`, `layouts/` (2), `config/` (3), `constants/` (1) — 4,184 LOC. Plus every `qk`/`invalidateQueries` call site app-wide (299 references across 307 files), because the map below is this session's deliverable.

### The map (the deliverable — F5–F22 check their writes against this)

**152 leaf keys · 127 `queryOptions` factories · 299 `qk` references** = 127 factory definitions, 104 invalidation sites, 65 component reads, 3 direct-cache (`setQueryData`).

The scanner that produced this was throwaway — the tables are the artefact. Re-derive with
`grep -rno "qk\.[A-Za-z0-9_.]*" public/js --include='*.ts*'`, then classify each hit by whether the six lines above it contain `invalidateQueries`/`removeQueries`/`invalidate:`.
